</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Vcn (8 bytes):** A signed integer representing a VCN within a data
stream. This value will always be greater than zero.

**Lcn (8 bytes):** A signed integer representing the LCN mapping to Vcn
in a data stream. This value will always be greater than zero.

**Length (8 bytes):** A signed integer representing the contiguous
length in clusters for which the VCN to LCN mapping holds. This value
will always be greater than zero.

**Properties (4 bytes):** A value representing the properties for this
VCN to LCN mapping. The value MUST be one of the following:

<table style="width:100%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 50%" />
<col style="width: 49%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_VALID</p>
<p>0x0010</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent is considered valid, where the VCN to LCN
mapping represents a written or zeroed extent.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_STREAM_RESERVED</p>
<p>0x0020</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent does not map to an LCN, but instead contains a
token representation an allocation reservation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_CRC32</p>
<p>0x0080</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent references data that is checksumed with the
CRC32 algorithm.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_CRC64</p>
<p>0x0100</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent references data that is checksumed with the
CRC64 algorithm.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_GHOSTED</p>
<p>0x0200</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent contains a ghosted recall buffer.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_READONLY</p>
<p>0x0400</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent is a cached copy of a different metadata extent.
This extent is immutable, and the LCN it references is not writable via
this extent.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>REFS_STREAM_EXTENT_PROPERTY_SPARSE</p>
<p>0x0008</p></td>
<td>The metadata extent represents a sparse range within the stream. The
range represented by this extent is analogous to a sparse hole in the
stream table.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.67 FSCTL_SET_COMPRESSION Request

The FSCTL_SET_COMPRESSION request message requests that the server set
the compression state of the file or directory associated with the
handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked. The
message contains a 16-bit unsigned integer.

The CompressionState element is as follows.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>

<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>

<tr>
<td colspan="16">CompressionState</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**CompressionState (2 bytes):** MUST be one of the following standard
values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 64%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>COMPRESSION_FORMAT_NONE</p>
<p>0x0000</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is not compressed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>COMPRESSION_FORMAT_DEFAULT</p>
<p>0x0001</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is compressed by using the default compression
algorithm.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_62" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_62">&lt;62&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>COMPRESSION_FORMAT_LZNT1</p>
<p>0x0002</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is compressed by using the LZNT1 compression
algorithm. For more information, see <a
href="https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=90549">[UASDC]</a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>All other values</td>
<td>Reserved for future use and MUST NOT be used.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

The actual file or directory compression performed when a server
receives a request for COMPRESSION_FORMAT_DEFAULT and
COMPRESSION_FORMAT_LZNT1 is
implementation-dependent.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_63"
class="anchor"></span>[\<63\>](#Appendix_A_63)

If the file system of the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) containing the
specified file or directory does not support per-file or per-directory
compression, the request MUST NOT succeed. The error code returned in
this situation is specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7).

### 2.3.68 FSCTL_SET_COMPRESSION Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_COMPRESSION
request](#Section_77f650a3e3a24a25baac4bf9b36bcc46).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The input buffer length is less than 2, or the handle is not to a
file or directory, or the requested CompressionState is not one of the
values listed in the table for <strong>CompressionState</strong> in
FSCTL_SET_COMPRESSION Request (section 2.3.67).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The <a
href="#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c"><strong>volume</strong></a>
does not allow compression.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_DISK_FULL</p>
<p>0xC00007F</p></td>
<td>The disk is full.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.69 FSCTL_SET_DEFECT_MANAGEMENT Request

Sets the software defect management state for the specified file
associated with the handle on which this FSCTL was invoked. Used for UDF
file systems.

This message contains a **FILE_SET_DEFECT_MGMT_BUFFER** structure.

**FILE_SET_DEFECT_MGMT_BUFFER** is defined as follows.

**Disable (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. If TRUE,
indicates that defect management will be disabled. If FALSE, indicates
that defect management will be enabled.

This FSCTL is valid only on UDF file systems. All other file systems
will treat this as an invalid FSCTL. For information regarding UDF, see
[\[UDF\]](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=184845).

### 2.3.70 FSCTL_SET_DEFECT_MANAGEMENT Reply

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned directly by the function that
processes this [**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is
STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are listed in the following
table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>An invalid parameter was passed to a service or function or the
handle on which this FSCTL was invoked is that of a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The specified request is not a valid operation for the target
device.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_SHARING_VIOLATION</p>
<p>0xC0000043</p></td>
<td>A file cannot be opened because the share access flags are
incompatible.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_VOLUME_DISMOUNTED</p>
<p>0xC000026E</p></td>
<td>An operation was attempted to a <a
href="#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c"><strong>volume</strong></a>
after it was dismounted.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_FILE_INVALID</p>
<p>0xC0000098</p></td>
<td>The volume for a file has been externally altered such that the
opened file is no longer valid.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_WRONG_VOLUME</p>
<p>0xC0000012</p></td>
<td>The wrong volume is in the drive.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_VERIFY_REQUIRED</p>
<p>0x80000016</p></td>
<td>The media has changed and a verify operation is in progress so no
reads or writes can be performed to the device, except those used in the
verify operation.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

There are no additional data elements in this reply.

### 2.3.71 FSCTL_SET_ENCRYPTION Request

The FSCTL_SET_ENCRYPTION request sets the encryption for the file or
directory associated with the given
handle.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_64"
class="anchor"></span>[\<64\>](#Appendix_A_64)<span id="Appendix_A_Target_65"
class="anchor"></span>[\<65\>](#Appendix_A_65)

The message contains an ENCRYPTION_BUFFER structure that indicates
whether to encrypt/decrypt a file or an individual stream.

**ENCRYPTION_BUFFER** is defined as follows.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>

<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>

<tr>

<td colspan="24">Padding</td>
</tr>

**EncryptionOperation (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer value that
indicates the operation to be performed. The valid values are as
follows.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 28%" />
<col style="width: 68%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SET_ENCRYPTION</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>This operation requests encryption of the specified file or
directory.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_66" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_66">&lt;66&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_CLEAR_ENCRYPTION</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>This operation requests removal of encryption from the specified
file or directory. It MUST fail if any streams for the file are marked
encrypted.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_67" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_67">&lt;67&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STREAM_SET_ENCRYPTION</p>
<p>0x00000003</p></td>
<td>This operation requests encryption of the specified stream.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_68" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_68">&lt;68&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STREAM_CLEAR_ENCRYPTION</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>This operation requests the removal of encryption from the specified
stream.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_69" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_69">&lt;69&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Private (1 byte):** An 8-bit unsigned char
value.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_70"
class="anchor"></span>[\<70\>](#Appendix_A_70)

**Padding (3 bytes):** These bytes MUST be ignored.

### 2.3.72 FSCTL_SET_ENCRYPTION Reply

This message returns the results of the FSCTL_SET_ENCRYPTION request. If
the file system of the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) containing the
specified file or directory does not support encryption, the request
MUST NOT succeed. The error code returned in this situation varies,
depending on the file system.

This message returns a status code, as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7), as well as a
[DECRYPTION_STATUS_BUFFER (section 2.3.72.1)](#Section_d02aafb50a9a4d35999063cc4a109849)
if an output buffer is passed in.

Upon success, the status code returned by the function that processes
this [**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is
STATUS_SUCCESS<span id="Appendix_A_Target_71"
class="anchor"></span>[\<71\>](#Appendix_A_71). The most common error
codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_MEDIA_WRITE_PROTECTED</p>
<p>0xC00000A2</p></td>
<td>The disk cannot be written to because it is write-protected.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The <strong>EncryptionOperation</strong> field value is invalid, the
open request is not for a file or directory or stream encryption has
been requested on a stream that is compressed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL</p>
<p>0xC0000023</p></td>
<td>The size of the input buffer is less than the size of the encryption
buffer structure defined in section <a
href="#Section_bf78ff7eb0a44ba988254af43682eb0d">2.3.71</a>, or an
output buffer is present and is smaller than a DECRYPTION_STATUS_BUFFER
structure.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_VOLUME_NOT_UPGRADED</p>
<p>0xC000029C</p></td>
<td>The version of the file system on the volume does not support
encryption.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_72" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_72">&lt;72&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The request was invalid for a system-specific reason.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_73" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_73">&lt;73&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_FILE_CORRUPT_ERROR</p>
<p>0xC0000102</p></td>
<td>A required attribute is missing from a directory for which
encryption was requested.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_74"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_74">&lt;74&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_VOLUME_DISMOUNTED</p>
<p>0xC000026E</p></td>
<td>The volume is not mounted.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_USER_BUFFER</p>
<p>0xC00000E8</p></td>
<td>An exception was raised while accessing a user buffer.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.73 FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION Request

The FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION Request message requests that the
server set the integrity state of the file or directory associated with
the handle on which this FSCTL was
invoked.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_75"
class="anchor"></span>[\<75\>](#Appendix_A_75)

If the file system of the volume containing the specified file or
directory does not support integrity, the request MUST NOT succeed. The
error code returned in this situation is specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7).

The FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_BUFFER element is as follows.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="16">ChecksumAlgorithm</td>
<td colspan="16">Reserved</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Flags</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**ChecksumAlgorithm (2 bytes):** For **ReFS v1**, the field MUST be set
to one of the following standard values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 29%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_NONE</p>
<p>0x0000</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is set to not use integrity.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_CRC64</p>
<p>0x0002</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is set to provide integrity using a CRC64
checksum.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_UNCHANGED</p>
<p>0xFFFF</p></td>
<td>The integrity status of the file or directory is unchanged.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>All other values</p>
<p>0x0003 — 0xFFFE</p></td>
<td>Reserved for future use and MUST NOT be used.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

For **ReFS v2**, the field MUST be set to one of the following standard
values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 29%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_NONE</p>
<p>0x0000</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is set to not use integrity.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_CRC32</p>
<p>0x0001</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is set to provide integrity using a CRC32 or
CRC64 checksum. If the ReFS cluster size is 4KB, the checksum used is
CRC32; otherwise, if the cluster size is 64K, the CRC64 checksum is
used.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_CRC64</p>
<p>0x0002</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is set to provide integrity using a CRC32 or
CRC64 checksum. If the ReFS cluster size is 4KB, the checksum used is
CRC32; otherwise, if the cluster size is 64K, the CRC64 checksum is
used.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>CHECKSUM_TYPE_UNCHANGED</p>
<p>0xFFFF</p></td>
<td>The integrity status of the file or directory is unchanged.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>All other values</p>
<p>0x0003 — 0xFFFE</p></td>
<td>Reserved for future use and MUST NOT be used.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

Note that for **ReFS v2** any value except CHECKSUM_TYPE_NONE or
CHECKSUM_TYPE_UNCHANGED will set the integrity value to a
file-system-selected integrity mechanism and is not guaranteed to use
the user specified checksum value.

**Reserved (2 bytes):** A 16-bit reserved value. This field MUST be set
to zero and MUST be ignored.

**Flags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains zero or
more of the following flag values. Flag values that are unspecified in
the following table SHOULD be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 53%" />
<col style="width: 43%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FSCTL_INTEGRITY_FLAG_CHECKSUM_ENFORCEMENT_OFF</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>When set, if a checksum does not match, the associated I/O operation
will not be failed.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.74 FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION
Request (section 2.3.73)](#Section_a4517cd53f5a4058a457bcff2baac011).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The input buffer length is less than the size, in bytes, of the
FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_BUFFER element; the handle is not to a
file or directory; or the requested <strong>ChecksumAlgorithm</strong>
field is not one of the values listed in the table for the
<strong>ChecksumAlgorithm</strong> field in the
FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION Request.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The volume does not support integrity.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_DISK_FULL</p>
<p>0xC000007F</p></td>
<td>The disk is full.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED</p>
<p>0xC00000BB</p></td>
<td>The file has been ghosted (allocation blocks are being shared).</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.75 FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_EX Request

The FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_EX Request message requests that the
server set the integrity state of the file or directory associated with
the handle on which this FSCTL was
invoked.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_76"
class="anchor"></span>[\<76\>](#Appendix_A_76)

If the file system of the volume containing the specified file or
directory does not support integrity, the request MUST NOT succeed. The
error code returned in this situation is specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7).

The **FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_BUFFER_EX** element is as follows.

**EnableIntegrity (1 byte):** This field MUST be one of the following
values:

| Value | Meaning |
|----|----|
| 0x00 | The file or directory is set to not use integrity. |
| 0x01 | The file or directory is set to provide integrity using CRC32 or CRC64 checksum. |

**A - KeepIntegrityStateUnchanged (1 byte):** This field MUST be one of
the following values:

| Value | Meaning |
|----|----|
| 0x00 | The file or directory integrity state should change based on the EnableIntegrity parameter. |
| 0x01 | The file or directory integrity state must not change. |

**Reserved1 (2 bytes):** A 16-bit reserved value. This field MUST be set
to zero and MUST be ignored.

**Flags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains zero or
more of the following flag values. Flag values that are unspecified in
the following table SHOULD be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 52%" />
<col style="width: 47%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FSCTL_INTEGRITY_FLAG_CHECKSUM_ENFORCEMENT_OFF</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>When set, if a checksum does not match, the associated I/O operation
will not be failed.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Version (1 byte):** An 8-bit value. This field MUST be set to 1.

**Reserved2 (7 bytes):** A 56-bit reserved value. This field MUST be set
to zero and MUST be ignored.

### 2.3.76 FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_EX Reply

This message returns the results of the
FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_EX Request (section
[2.3.75](#Section_e11a4bed4445494990b0d3bdbae36218)).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
**FSCTL** is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are listed in
the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 49%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The input buffer length is less than the size, in bytes, of the
FSCTL_SET_INTEGRITY_INFORMATION_BUFFER_EX element; the handle is not to
a file or directory; or Version is not equal to 1.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The volume does not support integrity.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_DISK_FULL</p>
<p>0xC000007F</p></td>
<td>The disk is full.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED</p>
<p>0xC00000BB</p></td>
<td>The file has been ghosted (allocation blocks are being shared).</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.77 FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID Request

This message sets the [**object
identifier**](#gt_aaaf2f1a-0b0a-487e-a0f0-c3510a6091b2) for the file or
directory associated with the handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked. The
message contains a
[FILE_OBJECTID_BUFFER (section 2.1.3)](#Section_5982df014b944feba6cd26a5eeaa9880)
data element. Either a Type 1 or a Type 2 buffer is
valid.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_77"
class="anchor"></span>[\<77\>](#Appendix_A_77)<span id="Appendix_A_Target_78"
class="anchor"></span>[\<78\>](#Appendix_A_78)

### 2.3.78 FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID
request](#Section_f4bd6d4ad2d8424e818394ffdf0ad258).

If the file system of the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) containing the
specified file or directory does not support the use of object IDs, the
request will not succeed. The error code returned in this situation
varies, depending on the file system.

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file or directory, or the input buffer's
length is not equal to the size of a <a
href="#Section_5982df014b944feba6cd26a5eeaa9880">FILE_OBJECTID_BUFFER</a>
structure.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened with write data or write attribute access
as well as restore access.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION</p>
<p>0xC0000035</p></td>
<td>The file or directory already has an object ID.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The file system does not support the use of object IDs.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_MEDIA_WRITE_PROTECTED</p>
<p>0xC00000A2</p></td>
<td>The volume is write-protected and changes to it cannot be made.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.79 FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID_EXTENDED Request

The FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID_EXTENDED request message requests that the
server set the extended information for the file or directory associated
with the handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked. The
message contains an EXTENDED_INFO data element.

The EXTENDED_INFO data element is defined as follows.

<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />

**ExtendedInfo (48 bytes):** A 48-byte binary large object(BLOB)
containing user-defined extended data that was passed to this FSCTL by
an application. In this situation, the user refers to the implementer
who is calling this FSCTL, meaning the extended info is opaque to
[**NTFS**](#gt_86f79a17-c0be-4937-8660-0cf6ce5ddc1a); there are no rules
enforced by NTFS as to what these last 48 bytes contain. Contrast this
with the first 16 bytes of an object ID, which can be used to open the
file, so NTFS requires that they be unique within a
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c).<span id="Appendix_A_Target_79"
class="anchor"></span>[\<79\>](#Appendix_A_79)

### 2.3.80 FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID_EXTENDED Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_OBJECT_ID_EXTENDED
request](#Section_757bc8faf24f44cf86dcadb2c113589d).

If the file system of the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) containing the
specified file or directory does not support the use of ObjectIds, the
request will not succeed. The error code returned in this situation
varies, depending on the file system.

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file or directory, or the input buffer's
length is not equal to the size of an EXTENDED_INFO structure.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened with write data or write attribute
access.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND</p>
<p>0xC0000034</p></td>
<td>The file or directory has no object ID.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The file system does not support the use of object IDs.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.81 FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT Request

This message requests that the server set a [**reparse
point**](#gt_4fed0b53-5fc8-4818-886f-93d87f3035e1) on the file or
directory associated with the handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked.

The message contains a
[REPARSE_GUID_DATA_BUFFER](#Section_a4d083740e9243e28f8888b94112f070) or
a [REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER](#Section_c3a420cb8a724adf87e8eee95379d78f)
(including subtypes) data element. Both the REPARSE_GUID_DATA_BUFFER and
REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER structures begin with a **ReparseTag** field. The
ReparseTag value uniquely identifies the
[**filter**](#gt_ffbe7b55-8e84-4f41-a18d-fc29191a4cda) driver that
creates/uses the reparse point, and the filter driver processes the
reparse point data as either a REPARSE_GUID_DATA_BUFFER or a
REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER, depending on the structure implemented by the
filter driver for that type of reparse point.

This message is applicable only to a file or directory handle, not to a
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) handle.

### 2.3.82 FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_REPARSE_POINT
request](#Section_4dc2b168f1774eeca14b25a51cbba2cf).

If the file system of the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) containing the
specified file or directory does not support [**reparse
points**](#gt_4fed0b53-5fc8-4818-886f-93d87f3035e1), the request will
not succeed. The error code returned in this situation varies, depending
on the file system.

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 35%" />
<col style="width: 64%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file or directory, or the output buffer's
length is greater than 0.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_IO_REPARSE_DATA_INVALID</p>
<p>0xC0000278</p></td>
<td>The input buffer length is less than the size of a <a
href="#Section_c3a420cb8a724adf87e8eee95379d78f">REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER</a>
structure, or the input buffer length is greater than 16,384, or a
REPARSE_DATA_BUFFER structure has been specified for a third party
reparse tag, or the GUID specified for a third party reparse tag does
not match the GUID known by the operating system for this reparse point,
or the reparse tag is 0 or 1.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The file system does not support reparse points.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.83 FSCTL_SET_SPARSE Request

This message requests that the server mark the file that is associated
with the handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked as
sparse. In a [**sparse
file**](#gt_57c7faf0-6002-4b1a-b8fc-fc9244d5a6e5), large ranges of zeros
(0) might not require disk allocation. Space for nonzero data is
allocated as the file is written. The message either has no data
elements at all or it contains a FILE_SET_SPARSE_BUFFER element. If
there is no data element, the sparse flag for the file is set, exactly
as if the FILE_SET_SPARSE_BUFFER element was supplied and had a
**SetSparse** value of TRUE.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_80"
class="anchor"></span>[\<80\>](#Appendix_A_80)

The **FILE_SET_SPARSE_BUFFER** element is as follows:

<colgroup>

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>

<th>0</th>

<th>7</th>

<th>9</th>

<p>0</p></th>

<td></td>
<td></td>

<td></td>

<td></td>
</tr>
</tbody>

**SetSparse (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value.

A FALSE value will cause the file system to attempt to "unsparse" the
file by allocating clusters for any regions of the file that are
currently sparsed. If the entire file is successfully unsparsed, the
sparse flag is cleared for the file. If an error is encountered during
unsparsing, any regions of the file that were unsparsed
MAY<span id="Appendix_A_Target_81"
class="anchor"></span>[\<81\>](#Appendix_A_81) remain unsparsed.

A TRUE value will cause the sparse flag for the file to set. Currently
allocated clusters SHOULD NOT<span id="Appendix_A_Target_82"
class="anchor"></span>[\<82\>](#Appendix_A_82) be deallocated.

### 2.3.84 FSCTL_SET_SPARSE Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_SPARSE
request](#Section_6a884fe53da14abb84c4f419d349d878).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 28%" />
<col style="width: 71%" />
</colgroup>

<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file, or the input buffer length is nonzero
and is less than the size of a FILE_SET_SPARSE_BUFFER structure.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle is not open with write data or write attribute
access.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.85 FSCTL_SET_ZERO_DATA Request

The FSCTL_SET_ZERO_DATA request message requests that the server fill
the specified range of the file (associated with the handle on which
this [**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked)
with zeros. The message contains a FILE_ZERO_DATA_INFORMATION element.

The FILE_ZERO_DATA_INFORMATION element is as follows.

</tr>

<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>

**FileOffset (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the file
offset of the start of the range to set to zeros, in bytes. The value of
this field MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**BeyondFinalZero (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
byte offset of the first byte beyond the last zeroed byte. The value of
this field MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

How an implementation zeros data within a file is
implementation-dependent. A file system MAY choose to deallocate regions
of disk space that have been zeroed.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_83"
class="anchor"></span>[\<83\>](#Appendix_A_83)

### 2.3.86 FSCTL_SET_ZERO_DATA Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_ZERO_DATA
request](#Section_6be5164f3eb64149b997c37df1444779).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 28%" />
<col style="width: 71%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file, or input buffer length is not equal to
the size of a FILE_ZERO_DATA_INFORMATION structure, or the given
<strong>FileOffset</strong> is less than zero, or the given
<strong>BeyondFinalZero</strong> is less than zero, or the given
<strong>FileOffset</strong> is greater than the given
BeyondFinalZero.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle is not open with write data or write attribute
access.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.87 FSCTL_SET_ZERO_ON_DEALLOCATION Request

This message requests that the server fill the clusters of the target
file with zeros when they are
deallocated.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_84"
class="anchor"></span>[\<84\>](#Appendix_A_84) This is used to set a
file to secure delete mode, which ensures that data will be zeroed upon
file truncation or deletion.

There are several side effects associated with this operation.

- If the file is resident, it is converted to non-resident and the
  resident portion is zeroed.

- When reallocating ranges of a compressed file, the clusters are both
  zeroed and then replaced with a cluster representing compressed zeros
  before being reallocated.

This message does not contain any additional data elements.

### 2.3.88 FSCTL_SET_ZERO_ON_DEALLOCATION Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SET_ZERO_ON_DEALLOCATION
request](#Section_e919ce3db6ab40c6a48e2c613911a5cf). The only data item
this message returns is a status code, as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 24%" />
<col style="width: 75%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>Zero on deallocation can only be set on a user file opened for write
access and cannot be set on a directory.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.89 FSCTL_SIS_COPYFILE Request

The FSCTL_SIS_COPYFILE request message requests that the server use the
[**single-instance storage
(SIS)**](#gt_40c57198-06d7-4dcb-a011-d8152b64d2cb)
[**filter**](#gt_ffbe7b55-8e84-4f41-a18d-fc29191a4cda) to copy a file.
The message contains an SI_COPYFILE data element.

If the SIS filter is installed on the server, it will attempt to copy
the specified source file to the specified destination file by creating
an SIS link instead of actually copying the file data. If necessary and
allowed, the source file is placed under SIS control before the
destination file is created.

This [**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) can be issued
against either a file or directory handle. The source and destination
files MUST reside on the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) associated with
the given handle.

The SI_COPYFILE data element is as follows.

including a terminating-Unicode null character.

**DestinationFileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the size, in bytes, of the **DestinationFileName** element,
including a terminating-Unicode null character.

**Flags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains zero or
more of the following flag values. Flag values not specified in the
following table SHOULD be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 23%" />
<col style="width: 76%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>COPYFILE_SIS_LINK</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td><p>If this flag is set, only create the destination file if the
source file is already under SIS control. If the source file is not
under SIS control, the FSCTL returns STATUS_OBJECT_TYPE_MISMATCH.</p>
<p>If this flag is not specified, place the source file under SIS
control (if it is not already under SIS control), and create the
destination file.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>COPYFILE_SIS_REPLACE</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td><p>If this flag is set, create the destination file if it does not
exist; if it does exist, overwrite it.</p>
<p>If this flag is not specified, create the destination file if it does
not exist; if it does exist, the FSCTL returns
STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION.</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**SourceFileName (variable):** A null-terminated Unicode string
containing the source file name.

**DestinationFileName (variable):** A null-terminated Unicode string
containing the destination file name.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_85"
class="anchor"></span>[\<85\>](#Appendix_A_85)

### 2.3.90 FSCTL_SIS_COPYFILE Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_SIS_COPYFILE
request](#Section_2ceb5108f6e4484ebe43863a16a5b69a).

The only data item this message returns is a status code, as specified
in section [2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon
success, the status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The input buffer is NULL, or the input buffer length is less than
the size of the SI_COPYFILE structure, or the given
<strong>SourceFileNameLength</strong> or
<strong>DestinationFileNameLength</strong> is less than 2 or greater
than the buffer length, or the given
<strong>SourceFileNameLength</strong> plus
<strong>DestinationFileNameLength</strong> is greater than the length of
the given <strong>SourceFileName</strong> plus
<strong>DestinationFileName</strong> in the input buffer, or the given
<strong>SourceFileName</strong> or <strong>DestinationFileName</strong>
is NULL, or the given <strong>SourceFileName</strong> or
<strong>DestinationFileName</strong> is not null-terminated.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND</p>
<p>0xC0000034</p></td>
<td>The source file does not exist.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION</p>
<p>0xC0000035</p></td>
<td>The COPYFILE_SIS_REPLACE flag was not specified, and the destination
file exists, or the source and destination file are the same.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_TYPE_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000024</p></td>
<td>The COPYFILE_SIS_LINK flag was specified, and the source file is not
under SIS control.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SAME_DEVICE</p>
<p>0xC00000D4</p></td>
<td>The source and destination file names are not located on the same <a
href="#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c"><strong>volume</strong></a>,
or the source and destination file names are located on the same volume,
but it is not the volume associated with the handle on which the FSCTL
was performed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The <a
href="#gt_40c57198-06d7-4dcb-a011-d8152b64d2cb"><strong>single-instance
storage (SIS)</strong></a> <a
href="#gt_ffbe7b55-8e84-4f41-a18d-fc29191a4cda"><strong>filter</strong></a>
is not installed on the server.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_FILE_IS_A_DIRECTORY</p>
<p>0xC00000BA</p></td>
<td>The source or destination file is a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The caller is not an administrator.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.3.91 FSCTL_VIRTUAL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY Request

This request contains a message with the same structure as the
IOCTL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY request (section
[2.8.1](#Section_02c896910a2e4e6c8d1e32c781406397)) with the following
values:

**PropertyId** **(4 bytes)**: 0x00000004

**QueryType** **(4 bytes)**: 0x00000000

Remote servers SHOULD ignore this
request.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_86"
class="anchor"></span>[\<86\>](#Appendix_A_86)

### 2.3.92 FSCTL_WRITE_USN_CLOSE_RECORD Request

This message requests that the server generate a record in the server's
file system change journal
[**stream**](#gt_f3529cd8-50da-4f36-aa0b-66af455edbb6) for the file or
directory associated with the handle on which this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) was invoked,
indicating that the file or directory was closed. This FSCTL can be
called independently of the actual file close operation to write a
[**USN**](#gt_01936446-8739-4b98-b83f-fb5e2a53ce4c) record and cause a
post of any pending USN updates for the indicated file.

No data structure is associated with this request.

### 2.3.93 FSCTL_WRITE_USN_CLOSE_RECORD Reply

This message returns the results of the [FSCTL_WRITE_USN_CLOSE_RECORD
request](#Section_059a4dea601f44a4b3687a7f5b668696) as a single field,
**Usn**, which is a 64-bit signed integer that contains the server file
system's [**USN**](#gt_01936446-8739-4b98-b83f-fb5e2a53ce4c) for the
file or directory. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

This message returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226) is STATUS_SUCCESS.
The most common error codes are listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The handle is not to a file or directory, or the length of the
output buffer is less than the size of a 64-bit integer, or the output
buffer does not begin on a 4-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The file system does not support the use of a USN change
journal.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## 2.4 File Information Classes

File information classes are numerical values (specified by the Level
column in the following table) that specify what information for a file
is to be queried or set or for local use<span id="Appendix_A_Target_87"
class="anchor"></span>[\<87\>](#Appendix_A_87). File information classes
can require additional information to be included in the query or the
response. When appropriate, the additional information is detailed in
the file information class description. The table indicates which file
information classes are supported for query and set
operations.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_88"
class="anchor"></span>[\<88\>](#Appendix_A_88)

| File information class | Level | Uses |
|----|----|----|
| [FileAccessInformation](#Section_01cf43d2deb340d3a39b9e68693d7c90) | 8 | Query |
| [FileAlignmentInformation](#Section_9b0b997185aa46518438f1c4298bcb0d) | 17 | Query |

| [FileAttributeTagInformation](#Section_d295752fce894b988553266d37c84f0e) | 35 | Query |

### 2.4.3 FileAlignmentInformation

This information class is used to query the buffer alignment required by
the underlying device.

A **FILE_ALIGNMENT_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

**AlignmentRequirement (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that MUST
contain one of the following values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 27%" />
<col style="width: 69%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>Specifies that there are no alignment requirements for the
device.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_WORD_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 2-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_LONG_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0x00000003</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 4-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_QUAD_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0x00000007</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on an 8-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_OCTA_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X0000000F</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 16-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_32_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X0000001F</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 32-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_64_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X0000003F</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 64-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_128_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X0000007F</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 128-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_256_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X000000FF</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 256-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_512_BYTE_ALIGNMENT</p>
<p>0X000001FF</p></td>
<td>Specifies that data MUST be aligned on a 512-byte boundary.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.4 FileAllocationInformation

This information class is used to set but not to query the allocation
size for a file. The file system is passed a 64-bit signed integer
containing the file allocation size, in bytes. The file system rounds
the requested allocation size up to an integer multiple of the cluster
size for nonresident files, or an implementation-defined multiple for
resident files.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_101"
class="anchor"></span>[\<101\>](#Appendix_A_101)<span id="Appendix_A_Target_102"
class="anchor"></span>[\<102\>](#Appendix_A_102) All unused allocation
(beyond EOF) is freed on the last handle close.

A FILE_ALLOCATION_INFORMATION data element, defined as follows, is
provided by the client.

**AllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
desired allocation to be used by the given file.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>

</table>

### 2.4.5 FileAlternateNameInformation

This information class is used to query [**alternate
name**](#gt_8b745eca-dc27-4455-a35d-bedef9fa29e7) information for a
file. The alternate name for a file is its
[**8.3**](#gt_d2302116-d3d3-4465-a72e-c07a7737b7ae) format name (eight
characters that appear before the "." and three characters that appear
after). A file MAY have an alternate name to achieve compatibility with
the 8.3 naming requirements of legacy
applications.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_103"
class="anchor"></span>[\<103\>](#Appendix_A_103)

A
[FILE_NAME_INFORMATION (section 2.1.7)](#Section_20406fb1605f4629ba9ac67ee25f23d2)
data element containing an 8.3 file name (section
[2.1.5.2.1](#Section_18e63b13ba434f5fa5b711e871b71f14)) is returned by
the server.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND</p>
<p>0xC0000034</p></td>
<td>The object name is not found or is empty.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before the complete name could be
returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**FileAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the file attributes. Valid file attributes are as specified in section
[2.6](#Section_ca28ec38f155476881d64bfeb8586fc9).

**ReparseTag (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies the
[**reparse point tag**](#gt_0be8a9c1-34d6-4632-b430-c5cf4ecc38a7). If
the **FileAttributes** member includes the FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT
attribute flag, this member specifies the reparse tag. Otherwise, this
member SHOULD be set to 0, and MUST be ignored. Section
[2.1.2.1](#Section_c8e77b3739094fe6a4ea2b9d423b1ee4) contains more
details on reparse tags.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>

<col style="width: 67%" />

</tr>

<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened to read file data or file attributes.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.7 FileBasicInformation

This information class is used to query or set file information.

A **FILE_BASIC_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server or provided by the client.

<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<td colspan="32">CreationTime</td>

<td colspan="32">...</td>

**CreationTime (8 bytes):** The time when the file was created; see
section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). A valid time
for this field is an integer greater than or equal to 0. When setting
file attributes, a value of 0 indicates to the server that it MUST NOT
change this attribute. When setting file attributes, a value of -1
indicates to the server that it MUST NOT change this attribute for all
subsequent operations on the same file handle. When setting file
attributes, a value of -2 indicates to the server that it MUST change
this attribute for all subsequent operations on the same file handle.
This field MUST NOT be set to a value less than
-2.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_104"
class="anchor"></span>[\<104\>](#Appendix_A_104)

**LastAccessTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was accessed; see
section 2.1.1. A valid time for this field is an integer greater than or
equal to 0. When setting file attributes, a value of 0 indicates to the
server that it MUST NOT change this attribute. When setting file
attributes, a value of -1 indicates to the server that it MUST NOT
change this attribute for all subsequent operations on the same file
handle. When setting file attributes, a value of -2 indicates to the
server that it MUST change this attribute for all subsequent operations
on the same file handle. This field MUST NOT be set to a value less than
-2.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_105"
class="anchor"></span>[\<105\>](#Appendix_A_105)

**LastWriteTime (8 bytes):** The last time information was written to
the file; see section 2.1.1. A valid time for this field is an integer
greater than or equal to 0. When setting file attributes, a value of 0
indicates to the server that it MUST NOT change this attribute. When
setting file attributes, a value of -1 indicates to the server that it
MUST NOT change this attribute for all subsequent operations on the same
file handle. When setting file attributes, a value of -2 indicates to
the server that it MUST change this attribute for all subsequent
operations on the same file handle. This field MUST NOT be set to a
value less than -2.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_106"
class="anchor"></span>[\<106\>](#Appendix_A_106)

**ChangeTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was changed; see
section 2.1.1. A valid time for this field is an integer greater than or
equal to 0. When setting file attributes, a value of 0 indicates to the
server that it MUST NOT change this attribute. When setting file
attributes, a value of -1 indicates to the server that it MUST NOT
change this attribute for all subsequent operations on the same file
handle. When setting file attributes, a value of -2 indicates to the
server that it MUST change this attribute for all subsequent operations
on the same file handle. This field MUST NOT be set to a value less than
-2.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_107"
class="anchor"></span>[\<107\>](#Appendix_A_107)

**FileAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the file attributes. Valid file attributes are specified in section
[2.6](#Section_ca28ec38f155476881d64bfeb8586fc9).

**Reserved (4 bytes):** A 32-bit field. This field is reserved. This
field can be set to any value, and MUST be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened to read file data or file attributes.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.8 FileBothDirectoryInformation

This information class is used in directory enumeration to return
detailed information about the contents of a directory.

This information class returns a list that contains a
**FILE_BOTH_DIR_INFORMATION** data element for each file or directory
within the target directory.

This information class differs from
[FileDirectoryInformation (section 2.4.10)](#Section_b38bf51890574c889ddd5e2d3976a64b)
in that it includes short names in the returns list.

When multiple **FILE_BOTH_DIR_INFORMATION** data elements are present in
the buffer, each MUST be aligned on an 8-byte boundary. Any bytes
inserted for alignment SHOULD be set to zero, and the receiver MUST
ignore them. No padding is required following the last data element.

A **FILE_BOTH_DIR_INFORMATION** data element is as follows.

<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">NextEntryOffset</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileIndex</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">CreationTime</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">LastAccessTime</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">LastWriteTime</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>

</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>

</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>

</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>

</tr>

</tr>
</tbody>

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the byte offset from the beginning of this entry, at which the next
FILE_BOTH_DIR_INFORMATION entry is located, if multiple entries are
present in a buffer. This member is zero if no other entries follow this
one. An implementation MUST use this value to determine the location of
the next entry (if multiple entries are present in a buffer).

**FileIndex (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
byte offset of the file within the parent directory. For file systems in
which the position of a file within the parent directory is not fixed
and can be changed at any time to maintain sort order, this field SHOULD
be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_108"
class="anchor"></span>[\<108\>](#Appendix_A_108)

**CreationTime (8 bytes):** The time when the file was created; see
section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). This value
MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**LastAccessTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was accessed; see
section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**LastWriteTime (8 bytes):** The last time information was written to
the file; see section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to
0.

**ChangeTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was changed; see
section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**EndOfFile (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
absolute new end-of-file position as a byte offset from the start of the
file. EndOfFile specifies the offset to the byte immediately following
the last valid byte in the file. Because this value is zero-based, it
actually refers to the first free byte in the file. That is, it is the
offset from the beginning of the file at which new bytes appended to the
file will be written. The value of this field MUST be greater than or
equal to 0.

**AllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
file allocation size, in bytes. The value of this field MUST be an
integer multiple of the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size.

**FileAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the file attributes. Valid file attributes are specified in section
[2.6](#Section_ca28ec38f155476881d64bfeb8586fc9).

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies
the length, in bytes, of the file name contained within the **FileName**
member.

**EaSize (4 bytes):** If **FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT** is set in the
**FileAttributes** field, this field MUST contain a reparse tag as
specified in section
[2.1.2.1](#Section_c8e77b3739094fe6a4ea2b9d423b1ee4). Otherwise, this
field is a 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the combined length, in
bytes, of the extended attributes (EA) for the file.

**ShortNameLength (1 byte):** An 8-bit signed integer that specifies the
length, in bytes, of the file name contained in the **ShortName**
member. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**Reserved (1 byte):** Reserved for alignment. This field can contain
any value and MUST be ignored.

**ShortName (24 bytes):** A sequence of Unicode characters containing
the short (8.3) file name. When working with this field, use
**ShortNameLength** to determine the length of the file name rather than
assuming the presence of a trailing null delimiter.

**FileName (variable):** A sequence of Unicode characters containing the
file name. When working with this field, use **FileNameLength** to
determine the length of the file name rather than assuming the presence
of a trailing null delimiter. [**Dot directory
names**](#gt_d45f0f94-9d52-490d-a8c4-2366a8008cb7) are valid for this
field. For more details, see section
[2.1.5.1](#Section_fccd0313036445bdb75c924fd6a5662f).

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.9 FileCompressionInformation

This information class is used to query compression information for a
file.

A FILE_COMPRESSION_INFORMATION data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server.

<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<tr>
<td colspan="32">CompressedFileSize</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>

<tr>
<td colspan="16">CompressionFormat</td>
<td colspan="8">CompressionUnitShift</td>
<td colspan="8">ChunkShift</td>

<td colspan="24">Reserved</td>
</tr>
</tbody>

**CompressedFileSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains
the size, in bytes, of the compressed file. This value MUST be greater
than or equal to 0.

**CompressionFormat (2 bytes):** A 16-bit unsigned integer that contains
the compression format. The actual compression operation associated with
each of these compression format values is implementation-dependent. An
implementation can link any local compression algorithm with the values
described in the following table because the compressed data does not
travel across the wire in the context of
[**FSCTL**](#gt_4ffb96a7-5fad-488e-9438-b7707d2e4226), FileInformation
class, or FileSystemInformation class requests or
replies.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_109"
class="anchor"></span>[\<109\>](#Appendix_A_109)

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 30%" />
<col style="width: 66%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>COMPRESSION_FORMAT_NONE</p>
<p>0x0000</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is not compressed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>COMPRESSION_FORMAT_LZNT1</p>
<p>0x0002</p></td>
<td>The file or directory is compressed by using the LZNT1 compression
algorithm.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>All other values</td>
<td>Reserved for future use.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**CompressionUnitShift (1 byte):** An 8-bit unsigned integer that
contains the [**compression unit
shift**](#gt_4259c4db-f267-4dfc-80a6-fa2306056a34), which is the number
of bits by which to left-shift a 1 bit to arrive at the [**compression
unit**](#gt_ce0d64e7-0847-4910-9611-b3044f3aae2d) size. The compression
unit size is the number of bytes in a compression unit, that is, the
number of bytes to be compressed. This value is
implementation-defined.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_110"
class="anchor"></span>[\<110\>](#Appendix_A_110)

**ChunkShift (1 byte):** An 8-bit unsigned integer that contains the
compression [**chunk**](#gt_6c1aef9a-ae5a-4c85-ac7c-84da5eaacbab) size
shift, which is the number of bits by which to left-shift a 1 bit to
arrive at the compression chunk size. The chunk size is the number of
bytes that the operating system's implementation of the Lempel-Ziv
compression algorithm tries to compress at one time. This value is
implementation-defined.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_111"
class="anchor"></span>[\<111\>](#Appendix_A_111)

**ClusterShift (1 byte):** An 8-bit unsigned integer that contains the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size shift,
which is the number of bits by which to left-shift a 1 bit to arrive at
the cluster size. The cluster size specifies the amount of space that is
saved by compression to successfully compress a compression unit. If a
cluster size amount of space is not saved by compression, the data in
that compression unit is stored uncompressed. Each successfully
compressed compression unit MUST occupy at least one cluster less than
the uncompressed compression unit. This value is
implementation-defined.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_112"
class="anchor"></span>[\<112\>](#Appendix_A_112)

**Reserved (3 bytes):** A 24-bit reserved value. This field SHOULD be
set to 0, and MUST be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The data was too large to fit into the specified buffer. No data is
returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.10 FileDirectoryInformation

This information class is used in directory enumeration to return
detailed information about the contents of a directory.

This information class returns a list that contains a
**FILE_DIRECTORY_INFORMATION** data element for each file or directory
within the target directory.

When multiple **FILE_DIRECTORY_INFORMATION** data elements are present
in the buffer, each MUST be aligned on an 8-byte boundary. Any bytes
inserted for alignment SHOULD be set to zero, and the receiver MUST
ignore them. No padding is required following the last data element.

A **FILE_DIRECTORY_INFORMATION** data element is as follows.

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the byte offset from the beginning of this entry, at which the next
FILE_DIRECTORY_INFORMATION entry is located, if multiple entries are
present in a buffer. This member MUST be zero if no other entries follow
this one. An implementation MUST use this value to determine the
location of the next entry (if multiple entries are present in a
buffer).

**FileIndex (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
byte offset of the file within the parent directory. For file systems in
which the position of a file within the parent directory is not fixed
and can be changed at any time to maintain sort order, this field SHOULD
be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_113"
class="anchor"></span>[\<113\>](#Appendix_A_113)

**CreationTime (8 bytes):** The time when the file was created; see
section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). This value
MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**LastAccessTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was accessed; see
section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**LastWriteTime (8 bytes):** The last time information was written to
the file; see section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to
0.

**ChangeTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was changed; see
section 2.1.1. This value MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**EndOfFile (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
absolute new end-of-file position as a byte offset from the start of the
file. EndOfFile specifies the offset to the byte immediately following
the last valid byte in the file. Because this value is zero-based, it
actually refers to the first free byte in the file. That is, it is the
offset from the beginning of the file at which new bytes appended to the
file will be written. The value of this field MUST be greater than or
equal to 0.

**AllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
file allocation size, in bytes. The value of this field MUST be an
integer multiple of the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size.

**FileAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the file attributes. Valid attributes are as specified in section
[2.6](#Section_ca28ec38f155476881d64bfeb8586fc9).

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies
the length, in bytes, of the file name contained within the **FileName**
member.

**FileName (variable):** A sequence of Unicode characters containing the
file name. When working with this field, use **FileNameLength** to
determine the length of the file name rather than assuming the presence
of a trailing null delimiter. [**Dot directory
names**](#gt_d45f0f94-9d52-490d-a8c4-2366a8008cb7) are valid for this
field. For more details, see section
[2.1.5.1](#Section_fccd0313036445bdb75c924fd6a5662f).

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**DeletePending (1 byte):** An 8-bit field that is set to 1 to indicate
that a file SHOULD be deleted when it is closed; otherwise, 0 which
means the file SHOULD NOT be deleted.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_114"
class="anchor"></span>[\<114\>](#Appendix_A_114)

For a discussion of file deletion semantics, see
[\[FSBO\]](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=140636).

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened with delete access.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_DIRECTORY_NOT_EMPTY</p>
<p>0xC0000101</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the directory trying to be deleted is not empty.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.12 FileDispositionInformationEx

This information class is used to mark a file for deletion.

A **FILE_DISPOSITION_INFORMATION_EX** data element, defined as follows,
is provided by the client.

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<p>0</p></th>
