**CreationTime (8 bytes):** The time when the file was created; see
section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). The value of
this field MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**LastAccessTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was accessed; see
section 2.1.1. The value of this field MUST be greater than or equal to
0.

**LastWriteTime (8 bytes):** The last time information was written to
the file; see section 2.1.1. The value of this field MUST be greater
than or equal to 0.

**ChangeTime (8 bytes):** The last time the file was changed; see
section 2.1.1. The value of this field MUST be greater than or equal to
0.

**AllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
file allocation size, in bytes. The value of this field MUST be an
integer multiple of the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size.

**EndOfFile (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
absolute new end-of-file position as a byte offset from the start of the
file. EndOfFile specifies the offset to the byte immediately following
the last valid byte in the file. Because this value is zero-based, it
actually refers to the first free byte in the file. That is, it is the
offset from the beginning of the file at which new bytes appended to the
file will be written. The value of this field MUST be greater than or
equal to 0.

**FileAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the file attributes. Valid attributes are as specified in section
[2.6](#Section_ca28ec38f155476881d64bfeb8586fc9).

**Reserved (4 bytes):** A 32-bit field. This field is reserved. This
field can be set to any value and MUST be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened to read file data or file attributes.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.35 FileNormalizedNameInformation

This information class is used to query the normalized name of a file. A
normalized name is an absolute pathname where each short name component
has been replaced with the corresponding long name component, and each
name component uses the exact letter casing stored on disk. This
information class returns a FILE_NAME_INFORMATION data element
containing an absolute pathname, as specified in section
[2.1.7](#Section_20406fb1605f4629ba9ac67ee25f23d2).
<span id="Appendix_A_Target_139"
class="anchor"></span>[\<139\>](#Appendix_A_139)

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 50%" />
<col style="width: 49%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error Code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED</p>
<p>0xC00000BB</p></td>
<td>The resource is not supported.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before the complete name could be
returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.36 FileObjectIdInformation

This information class is used locally to query object ID information
for the files in a directory on a
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c). The query MUST
fail if the file system does not support object
IDs.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_140"
class="anchor"></span>[\<140\>](#Appendix_A_140)

The data returned to the caller will take one of two forms. The choice
of which data structure to use, and the interpretation of the data
within it, is application-specific. An application implementer chooses
one of the following two data elements as the structure for its object
ID information data.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_141"
class="anchor"></span>[\<141\>](#Appendix_A_141)

- [FILE_OBJECTID_INFORMATION_TYPE_1 (section 2.4.36.1)](#Section_63cdde1685ac480c95bf0bb8f5f09de8).

- [FILE_OBJECTID_INFORMATION_TYPE_2 (section 2.4.36.2)](#Section_42fa165135a54cdd94fc881917b714b2).

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The target file system does not implement this functionality.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS</p>
<p>0xC0000003</p></td>
<td>The specified information class is not a valid information class for
the specified object.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The file specified is not a valid parameter.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NO_SUCH_FILE</p>
<p>0xC000000F</p></td>
<td>The file does not exist.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NO_MORE_FILES</p>
<p>0x80000006</p></td>
<td>No more files were found which match the file specification.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before all of the
<strong>ObjectID</strong> information could be returned. Only complete
<strong>FILE_OBJECTID_INFORMATION</strong> structures are returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

#### 2.4.36.1 FILE_OBJECTID_INFORMATION_TYPE_1

A **FILE_OBJECTID_INFORMATION_TYPE_1** data element is as follows.

**FileReferenceNumber (8 bytes):** The 64-bit file ID, as specified in
section [2.1.9](#Section_2d3333fefc984a6f98a24bb805aff407), for the
file. For file systems that do not support a 64-bit file ID, this field
MUST be set to 0, and MUST be ignored.

**ObjectId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte
[**GUID**](#gt_f49694cc-c350-462d-ab8e-816f0103c6c1) that uniquely
identifies the file or directory within the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) on which it
resides. Specifically, the same object ID can be assigned to another
file or directory on a different volume, but it MUST NOT be assigned to
another file or directory on the same volume.

**BirthVolumeId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte GUID that uniquely identifies
the volume on which the object resided when the object identifier was
created, or zero if the volume had no object identifier at that time.
After copy operations, move operations, or other file operations, this
might not be the same as the object identifier of the volume on which
the object presently resides.

**BirthObjectId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte GUID value containing the object
identifier of the object at the time it was created. After copy
operations, move operations, or other file operations, this value might
not be the same as the **ObjectId** member at
present.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_142"
class="anchor"></span>[\<142\>](#Appendix_A_142)

**DomainId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte GUID value containing the domain
identifier. This value is unused; it SHOULD be zero and MUST be ignored.

<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileReferenceNumber</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">ObjectId (16 bytes)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">ExtendedInfo (48 bytes)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**FileReferenceNumber (8 bytes):** The 64-bit file ID, as specified in
section 2.1.9, for the file. For file systems that do not support a
64-bit file ID, this field MUST be set to 0, and MUST be ignored.

**ObjectId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte
[**GUID**](#gt_f49694cc-c350-462d-ab8e-816f0103c6c1) that uniquely
identifies the file or directory within the
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) on which it
resides. Specifically, the same object ID can be assigned to another
file or directory on a different volume, but it MUST NOT be assigned to
another file or directory on the same volume.

**ExtendedInfo (48 bytes):** A 48-byte BLOB that contains
application-specific extended information on the file object. If no
extended information has been written for this file, the server MUST
return 48 bytes of 0x00 in this field.

### 2.4.37 FilePipeInformation

This information class is used to query or set information on a named
pipe that is not specific to one end of the pipe or another.

A **FILE_PIPE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server or provided by the client.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

</colgroup>

<th>0</th>

<th>7</th>

<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>

<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>

<p>0</p></th>

</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">ReadMode</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">CompletionMode</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**ReadMode (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that MUST contain one
of the following values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 31%" />
<col style="width: 66%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_MODE</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, data MUST be read from the pipe as a
stream of bytes.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_MESSAGE_MODE</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, data MUST be read from the pipe as a
stream of messages.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

If this field is set to FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_MODE, any attempt to
subsequently change it MUST fail with a STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER error
code.

**CompletionMode (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that MUST
contain one of the following values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 64%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_QUEUE_OPERATION</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, blocking mode MUST be enabled. When the
pipe is being connected, read to, or written from, the operation is not
completed until there is data to read, all data is written, or a client
is connected. Use of this mode can result in the server waiting
indefinitely for a client process to perform an action.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_COMPLETE_OPERATION</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, non-blocking mode MUST be enabled. When
the pipe is being connected, read to, or written from, the operation
completes immediately.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td><p>An invalid parameter was passed to a service or function. When
setting the FilePipeInformation information level,
STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER will be returned:</p>
<ul>
<li><p>If the <strong>ReadMode</strong> field is set to
FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_MODE and a subsequent set operation attempts to
set the <strong>ReadMode</strong> field to any value other than
FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_MODE.</p></li>
<li><p>If the value of the <strong>ReadMode</strong> field is not equal
to FILE_PIPE_MESSAGE_MODE or FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_MODE.</p></li>
<li><p>If the value of the <strong>CompletionMode</strong> field is not
equal to FILE_PIPE_QUEUE_OPERATION or
FILE_PIPE_COMPLETE_OPERATION.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

For more information on named pipes, please see
[\[PIPE\]](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=90247).

### 2.4.38 FilePipeLocalInformation

This information class is used to query information on a named pipe that
is associated with the end of the pipe that is being queried.

A **FILE_PIPE_LOCAL_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<td colspan="32">InboundQuota</td>
</tr>

<td colspan="32">ReadDataAvailable</td>
</tr>

<td colspan="32">NamedPipeState</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">NamedPipeEnd</td>
</tr>
</tbody>

**NamedPipeType (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
named pipe type. MUST be one of the following.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 30%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_BYTE_STREAM_TYPE</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, data MUST be read from the pipe as a <a
href="#gt_f3529cd8-50da-4f36-aa0b-66af455edbb6"><strong>stream</strong></a>
of bytes.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_MESSAGE_TYPE</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>If this flag is specified, data MUST be read from the pipe as a
stream of messages.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**NamedPipeConfiguration (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the named pipe configuration. MUST be one of the following.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 24%" />
<col style="width: 72%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_INBOUND</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, the flow of data in the pipe goes from
client to server only.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_OUTBOUND</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, the flow of data in the pipe goes from
server to client only.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_FULL_DUPLEX</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>If this value is specified, the pipe is bi-directional; both server
and client processes can read from and write to the pipe.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**MaximumInstances (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the maximum number of instances that can be created for this pipe.

**CurrentInstances (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the number of current named pipe instances.

**InboundQuota (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
inbound quota, in bytes, for the named pipe. The inbound quota is the
size of the buffer reserved for inbound transfer of data on the pipe.

**ReadDataAvailable (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the bytes of data available to be read from the named pipe.

**OutboundQuota (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
outbound quota, in bytes, for the named pipe. The outbound quota is the
size of the buffer reserved for outbound transfer of data on the pipe.

**WriteQuotaAvailable (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the write quota, in bytes, for the named pipe. If the
**NamedPipeEnd** field is set to FILE_PIPE_CLIENT_END, the
**WriteQuotaAvailable** field is the remaining **InboundQuota** field
available. If the **NamedPipeEnd** field is set to FILE_PIPE_SERVER_END,
the **WriteQuotaAvailable** field is the remaining **OutboundQuota**
field available.

**NamedPipeState (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the named pipe state that specifies the connection status for the named
pipe. MUST be one of the following.

<table style="width:77%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 44%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_DISCONNECTED_STATE</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>Named pipe is disconnected.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_LISTENING_STATE</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>Named pipe is waiting to establish a connection.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_CONNECTED_STATE</p>
<p>0x00000003</p></td>
<td>Named pipe is connected.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_CLOSING_STATE</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>Named pipe is in the process of being closed.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**NamedPipeEnd (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
type of the named pipe end, which specifies whether this is the client
or the server side of a named pipe. MUST be one of the following.

<table style="width:60%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 23%" />
<col style="width: 36%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_CLIENT_END</p>
<p>0x00000000</p></td>
<td>This is the client end of a named pipe.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PIPE_SERVER_END</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>This is the server end of a named pipe.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

For more information on named pipes, please see
[\[PIPE\]](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=90247).

### 2.4.39 FilePipeRemoteInformation

This information class is used to query information on a named pipe that
is associated with the client end of the pipe that is being queried.
Remote information is not available for local pipes or for the server
end of a remote pipe. Therefore, this information class is usable only
by the client to retrieve information associated with its end of the
pipe.

A **FILE_PIPE_REMOTE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server.

**CollectDataTime (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that MUST contain
the maximum amount of time counted in 100-nanosecond intervals that will
elapse before transmission of data from the client machine to the
server.

**MaximumCollectionCount (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
MUST contain the maximum size, in bytes, of data that will be collected
on the client machine before transmission to the server.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>

that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

[\[PIPE\]](https://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=90247).

### 2.4.40 FilePositionInformation

**CurrentByteOffset (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that MUST
contain the offset, in bytes, of the file pointer from the beginning of
the file. A unique offset value is maintained for each open of a file.
When setting the position, only values greater than or equal to zero are
valid. If the given file was opened using the
FILE_NO_INTERMEDIATE_BUFFERING flag, the offset that is being set SHOULD
be aligned to a sector boundary. This value
SHOULD<span id="Appendix_A_Target_144"
class="anchor"></span>[\<144\>](#Appendix_A_144) be updated by read and
write operations if the given file was opened using the
FILE_SYNCHRONOUS_IO_ALERT or FILE_SYNCHRONOUS_IO_NONALERT flags.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>Returned when setting the offset if the
<strong>CurrentByteOffset</strong> is negative or the file was opened
using the FILE_NO_INTERMEDIATE_BUFFERING flag and
<strong>CurrentByteOffset</strong> is not aligned to a sector
boundary.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.41 FileQuotaInformation

This information class is used to query or to set file quota information
for a [**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c). For
queries, an optional buffer of
[FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION (section 2.4.41.1)](#Section_56adae21add4443497ece40e87739d52)
data elements is provided by the client to specify the
[**SID**](#gt_83f2020d-0804-4840-a5ac-e06439d50f8d)s for which quota
information is requested. If the **FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION** buffer
is not specified, information for all quotas is returned. A buffer of
**FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data elements is returned by the server. For
sets, **FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data elements are populated and sent by
the client, as specified in
[\[MS-SMB\]](%5bMS-SMB%5d.pdf#Section_f210069c70864dc2885e861d837df688)
section 2.2.7.6.1 and
[\[MS-SMB2\]](%5bMS-SMB2%5d.pdf#Section_5606ad475ee0437a817e70c366052962)
section 3.2.4.15.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_145"
class="anchor"></span>[\<145\>](#Appendix_A_145)

When multiple **FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data elements are present in
the buffer, each MUST be aligned on an 8-byte boundary. Any bytes
inserted for alignment SHOULD be set to zero, and the receiver MUST
ignore them. No padding is required following the last data element.

A **FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data element is as follows.

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the byte offset from the beginning of this entry, at which the next
FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION entry is located, if multiple entries are present
in a buffer. This member MUST be zero if no other entries follow this
one. An implementation MUST use this value to determine the location of
the next entry (if multiple entries are present in a buffer).

**SidLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
length, in bytes, of the **Sid** data element.

**ChangeTime (8 bytes):** The last time that the quota was changed; see
section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). This value
MUST be greater than or equal to 0x0000000000000000. When setting quota
information, the server MUST ignore the value of this field.

**QuotaUsed (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
amount of quota used by this user, in bytes. This value MUST be greater
than or equal to 0x0000000000000000. When setting quota information, the
server MUST ignore the value of this field.

**QuotaThreshold (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
[**disk quota**](#gt_82dfddec-0095-4f51-9b36-b6f5a0458e22) warning
threshold, in bytes, on this volume for this user. This field MUST be
set to a 64-bit integer value greater than or equal to 0 to set a quota
warning threshold for this user on this volume. If this field is set to
-1 there is no quota warning threshold for this user.

**QuotaLimit (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the disk
quota limit, in bytes, on this volume for this user. This field MUST be
set to a 64-bit integer value greater than or equal to zero to set a
disk quota limit for this user on this volume, to -1 to specify that no
quota limit is set for this user, or to -2 to delete the quota entry for
the user.

**Sid (variable):** Security identifier (SID) for this user.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The target file system does not implement this functionality.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS</p>
<p>0xC0000003</p></td>
<td>The specified information class is not a valid information class for
the specified object.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The SID or SID Length specified is not a valid parameter.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NO_SUCH_FILE</p>
<p>0xC000000F</p></td>
<td>For query operations, indicates that no
<strong>FILE_QUOTA_INFORMATION</strong> data elements were returned that
matched the input criteria.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL</p>
<p>0xC0000023</p></td>
<td>The buffer is too small to contain the entry. No information has
been written to the buffer.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

#### 2.4.41.1 FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION

This structure is used to provide the list of
[**SIDs**](#gt_83f2020d-0804-4840-a5ac-e06439d50f8d) for which quota
query information is requested.

When multiple **FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data elements are present
in the buffer, each MUST be aligned on a 4-byte boundary. Any bytes
inserted for alignment SHOULD be set to zero, and the receiver MUST
ignore them. No padding is required following the last data element.

A **FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION** data element is as follows.

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the byte offset from the beginning of this entry, at which the next
FILE_GET_QUOTA_INFORMATION entry is located, if multiple entries are
present in a buffer. This member MUST be zero if no other entries follow
this one. An implementation MUST use this value to determine the
location of the next entry (if multiple entries are present in a
buffer).

**SidLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
length, in bytes, of the **Sid** data element.

**Sid (variable):** SID for this user. SIDs are sent in little-endian
format and require no padding. The format of a SID is as specified in
[\[MS-DTYP\]](%5bMS-DTYP%5d.pdf#Section_cca2742956894a16b2b49325d93e4ba2)
section 2.4.2.2.

### 2.4.42 FileRenameInformation

This information class is used to rename a file. The data element
provided by the client takes one of two forms, depending on whether it
is embedded within SMB or SMB2. The structure definitions are as
follows:

- FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION_TYPE_1 for the SMB protocol (section
  [2.4.42.1](#Section_3668ae461df54656b481763877428bcb)).

- FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION_TYPE_2 for the SMB2 protocol (section
  [2.4.42.2](#Section_52aa0b7080944971862d79793f41e6a8)).

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table style="width:100%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 33%" />
<col style="width: 66%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>An invalid parameter was passed for <strong>FileName</strong> or
<strong>FileNameLength</strong>, or the <strong>RootDirectory</strong>
field value was nonzero for a network operation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened with delete access, or the target file was
open and <strong>ReplaceIfExists</strong> is nonzero.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SAME_DEVICE</p>
<p>0xC00000D4</p></td>
<td>The destination file of a rename request is located on a different
device than the source of the rename request.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_INVALID</p>
<p>0xC0000033</p></td>
<td>The object name is invalid for the target file system.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION</p>
<p>0xC0000035</p></td>
<td>The specified name already exists and
<strong>ReplaceIfExists</strong> is zero.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

#### 2.4.42.1 FileRenameInformation for SMB

This information class is used to rename a file from within the SMB
Protocol, as specified in
[\[MS-SMB\]](%5bMS-SMB%5d.pdf#Section_f210069c70864dc2885e861d837df688).

A **FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION_TYPE_1** data element, defined as follows,

**ReplaceIfExists (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. Set to TRUE to
indicate that if a file with the given name already exists, it SHOULD be
replaced with the given file. Set to FALSE to indicate that the rename
operation MUST fail if a file with the given name already exists.

**Reserved (3 bytes):** Reserved area for alignment. This field can
contain any value and MUST be ignored.

**RootDirectory (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
file handle for the directory to which the new name of the file is
relative. For network operations, this value MUST be zero.

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies
the length, in bytes, of the file name contained within the **FileName**
field.

**FileName (variable):** A sequence of [**Unicode
characters**](#gt_fd33af2e-e1ce-4f8e-a706-f9fb8123f9b0) containing the
new file name of type **Filename** (section
[2.1.5.2](#Section_2917da5c253c4c0eaaf69dddc37d2e6e)). When working with
this field, use **FileNameLength** to determine the length of the file
name rather than assuming the presence of a trailing null delimiter.

**ReplaceIfExists (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. Set to TRUE to
indicate that if a file with the given name already exists, it SHOULD be
replaced with the given file. Set to FALSE to indicate that the rename
operation MUST fail if a file with the given name already exists.

**Reserved (7 bytes):** Reserved area for alignment. This field can
contain any value and MUST be ignored.

**RootDirectory (8 bytes):** A 64-bit unsigned integer that contains the
file handle for the directory to which the new name of the file is
relative. For network operations, this value MUST always be zero.

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies
the length, in bytes, of the file name contained within the **FileName**
field.

**FileName (variable):** A sequence of [**Unicode
characters**](#gt_fd33af2e-e1ce-4f8e-a706-f9fb8123f9b0) containing the
new name of the file. When working with this field, use
**FileNameLength** to determine the length of the file name rather than
assuming the presence of a trailing null delimiter.

**Padding (variable):** Length of this field MUST be the number of bytes
required to make the size of this structure at least 24. This field MAY
be set to 0 and MUST be ignored on receipt.

<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Flags</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Reserved</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">RootDirectory</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileNameLength</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileName (variable)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Padding (variable)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Flags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit field that specifies options on how the
file is renamed.

This field contains one or more of the values in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 49%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>If set, indicates that if a file with the given name already exists,
it SHOULD be replaced with the given file. If not set, indicates that
the rename operation MUST fail if a file with the given name already
exists.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_POSIX_SEMANTICS</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>If set and FILE_RENAME_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS is set, indicates that if a
file with the given name already exists the file SHOULD be deleted using
POSIX-style semantics. Existing handles to the replaced file continue to
be valid. Any subsequent opens of the target name will open the renamed
file, not the replaced file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_PIN_STATE_INHERITANCE</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>If set, when renaming a file to a new directory, suppress any
inheritance rules related to the FILE_ATTRIBUTE_PINNED and
FILE_ATTRIBUTE_UNPINNED attributes.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_146"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_146">&lt;146&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_STORAGE_RESERVE_INHERITANCE</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>If set, when renaming a file to a new directory, it suppresses any
inheritance rules related to the storage reserve ID property of the
file.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_147" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_147">&lt;147&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_NO_INCREASE_AVAILABLE_SPACE</p>
<p>0x00000010</p></td>
<td>If set and FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_STORAGE_RESERVE_INHERITANCE is not
set; when renaming a file to a new directory, automatically resize
affected storage reserve areas as needed to prevent the user visible
free space on the volume from increasing. Requires manage volume
access.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_148" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_148">&lt;148&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_NO_DECREASE_AVAILABLE_SPACE</p>
<p>0x00000020</p></td>
<td>if set and FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_STORAGE_RESERVE_INHERITANCE is not
set; when renaming a file to a new directory, automatically resize
affected storage reserve areas as needed to prevent the user visible
free space on the volume from decreasing. Requires manage volume
access.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_149" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_149">&lt;149&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_PRESERVE_AVAILABLE_SPACE</p>
<p>0x00000030</p></td>
<td>Equivalent to specifying both
FILE_RENAME_NO_INCREASE_AVAILABLE_SPACE and
FILE_RENAME_NO_DECREASE_AVAILABLE_SPACE.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_150"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_150">&lt;150&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_IGNORE_READONLY_ATTRIBUTE</p>
<p>0x00000040</p></td>
<td>If set and FILE_RENAME_REPLACE_IF_EXISTS is set; allow replacing a
file even if the read-only attribute is set on the file.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_151" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_151">&lt;151&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_FORCE_RESIZE_TARGET_SR</p>
<p>0x00000080</p></td>
<td>If set and FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_STORAGE_RESERVE_INHERITANCE is not
set; when renaming a file to a new directory that is part of a different
storage reserve area, always grow the target directory's storage reserve
area by the full size of the file being renamed. Requires manage volume
access.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_152" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_152">&lt;152&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_FORCE_RESIZE_SOURCE_SR</p>
<p>0x00000100</p></td>
<td>If set and FILE_RENAME_SUPPRESS_STORAGE_RESERVE_INHERITANCE is not
set; when renaming a file to a new directory that is part of a different
storage reserve area, always shrink the source directory's storage
reserve area by the full size of the file being renamed. Requires manage
volume access.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_153" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_153">&lt;153&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RENAME_FORCE_RESIZE_SR</p>
<p>0x00000180</p></td>
<td>Equivalent to specifying both FILE_RENAME_FORCE_RESIZE_TARGET_SR and
FILE_RENAME_FORCE_RESIZE_SOURCE_SR.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_154"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_154">&lt;154&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Reserved (4 bytes):** Reserved area for alignment. This field can
contain any value and MUST be ignored.

**RootDirectory (8 bytes):** A 64-bit unsigned integer that contains the
file handle for the directory to which the new name of the file is
relative. For network operations, this value MUST always be zero.

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that specifies
the length, in bytes, of the file name contained within the **FileName**
field.

**FileName (variable):** A sequence of [**Unicode
characters**](#gt_fd33af2e-e1ce-4f8e-a706-f9fb8123f9b0) containing the
new name of the file. When working with this field, use
**FileNameLength** to determine the length of the file name rather than
assuming the presence of a trailing null delimiter.

**Padding (variable):** Length of this field MUST be the number of bytes
required to make the size of this structure at least 24. This field MAY
be set to 0 and MUST be ignored on receipt.

**FileReferenceNumber (8 bytes):** The 64-bit file ID, as specified in
section 2.1.9, for the file.

**Tag (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer value containing the
[**reparse point tag**](#gt_0be8a9c1-34d6-4632-b430-c5cf4ecc38a7) that
uniquely identifies the owner of the reparse point. Section
[2.1.2.1](#Section_c8e77b3739094fe6a4ea2b9d423b1ee4) contains more
details on reparse tags.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 34%" />
<col style="width: 65%" />

that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_DEVICE_REQUEST</p>
<p>0xC0000010</p></td>
<td>The target file system does not implement this functionality.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS</p>
<p>0xC0000003</p></td>
<td>The specified information class is not a valid information class for
the specified object.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NO_SUCH_FILE</p>
<p>0xC000000F</p></td>
<td>No reparse points exist for the given file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before all of the
FILE_REPARSE_POINT_INFORMATION structures could be returned; a partial
structure might be returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.45 FileSfioReserveInformation

This information class is used locally to query or set reserved
bandwidth for a file handle. Conceptually reserving bandwidth is
effectively specifying the bytes per second to allocate to file IO.

A **FILE_SFIO_RESERVE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned to the caller.

<th>6</th>

</tr>

</tr>

**RequestsPerPeriod (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer indicating
the number of I/O requests that complete per period of time, as
specified in the **Period** field. When setting bandwidth reservation, a
value of 0 indicates to the file system that it MUST free any existing
reserved bandwidth.

**Period (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the period
for reservation, which is the time from which I/O is issued to the
kernel until the time the I/O is completed, specified in milliseconds.

**RetryFailures (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value.

**Discardable (1 byte):** A Boolean (section 2.1.8) value.

**Reserved (2 bytes):** Reserved for alignment. This field can contain
any value and MUST be ignored.

**RequestSize (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that indicates the
minimum size of any individual I/O request that can be issued by an
application using bandwidth reservation. When setting reservations, this
field MUST be ignored by servers and SHOULD be set to 0 by clients.

**NumOutstandingRequests (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
indicates the number of RequestSize I/O requests allowed to be
outstanding at any time. When setting reservations, this field MUST be
ignored by servers and SHOULD be set to 0 by clients.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED</p>
<p>0xC00000BB</p></td>
<td>The request is not supported.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.46 FileShortNameInformation

This information class is used to change a file's [**short
name**](#gt_0e05d13e-4bda-44f7-8650-e1c1b53cca17). If the supplied name
is of zero length, the file's existing short name, if any,
SHOULD<span id="Appendix_A_Target_155"
class="anchor"></span>[\<155\>](#Appendix_A_155) be deleted. Otherwise,
the supplied name MUST be a valid short name as specified in section
[2.1.5.2.1](#Section_18e63b13ba434f5fa5b711e871b71f14) and be unique
among all file names and short names in the same directory as the file
being operated on. A caller changing the file's short name MUST have
SeRestorePrivilege, as specified in
[\[MS-LSAD\]](%5bMS-LSAD%5d.pdf#Section_1b5471ef4c334a91b079dfcbb82f05cc)
section 3.1.1.2.1.

A
[FILE_NAME_INFORMATION (section 2.1.7)](#Section_20406FB1605F4629BA9AC67EE25F23D2)
data element containing an 8.3 file name (section 2.1.5.2.1) is provided
by the client.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 49%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_MEDIA_WRITE_PROTECTED</p>
<p>0xC00000A2</p></td>
<td>The target cannot be written to because it is write-protected.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The file name is not a valid parameter.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_ACCESS_DENIED</p>
<p>0xC0000022</p></td>
<td>The handle was not opened to write file data or file attributes, or
the file has been deleted.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD</p>
<p>0xC0000061</p></td>
<td>The SeRestorePrivilege privilege is not held.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_SHORT_NAMES_NOT_ENABLED_ON_VOLUME</p>
<p>0xC000019F</p></td>
<td>Short names are not enabled on this volume.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_COLLISION</p>
<p>0xC0000035</p></td>
<td>The specified name already exists.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

This information class is used to query file information.

A **FILE_STANDARD_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is

**AllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
file allocation size, in bytes. The value of this field MUST be an
integer multiple of the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size.

**EndOfFile (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
absolute end-of-file position as a byte offset from the start of the
file. **EndOfFile** specifies the offset to the byte immediately
following the last valid byte in the file. Because this value is
zero-based, it actually refers to the first free byte in the file. That
is, it is the offset from the beginning of the file at which new bytes
appended to the file will be written. The value of this field MUST be
greater than or equal to 0.

**NumberOfLinks (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains the
number of non-deleted links to this file.

**DeletePending (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. Set to TRUE to
indicate that a file deletion has been requested; set to FALSE
otherwise.

**Directory (1 byte):** A Boolean (section 2.1.8) value. Set to TRUE to
indicate that the file is a directory; set to FALSE otherwise.

**Reserved (2 bytes):** A 16-bit field. This field is reserved. This
field can be set to any value, and MUST be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.48 FileStandardLinkInformation

This information class is used locally to query file link
information.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_156"
class="anchor"></span>[\<156\>](#Appendix_A_156)

A **FILE_STANDARD_LINK_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows,
is returned to the caller.

<td colspan="8">DeletePending</td>
<td colspan="8">Directory</td>
<td colspan="16">Reserved</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**NumberOfAccessibleLinks (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the number of non-deleted links to this file.

**TotalNumberOfLinks (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the total number of links to this file, including links marked
for delete.

**DeletePending (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value that MUST be
set to TRUE to indicate that a file deletion has been requested;
otherwise, FALSE.

**Directory (1 byte):** An 8-bit field that MUST be set to 1 to indicate
that the file is a directory; otherwise, 0.

**Reserved (2 bytes):** A 16-bit field. This field is reserved. This
field can be set to any value and MUST be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 33%" />
<col style="width: 66%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED</p>
<p>0xC00000BB</p></td>
<td>The request is not supported.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.49 FileStreamInformation

This information class is used to enumerate the data
[**streams**](#gt_f3529cd8-50da-4f36-aa0b-66af455edbb6) of a file or a
directory. A buffer of **FILE_STREAM_INFORMATION** data elements is
returned by the server.

When multiple **FILE_STREAM_INFORMATION** data elements are present in
the buffer, each MUST be aligned on an 8-byte boundary; any bytes
inserted for alignment SHOULD be set to zero and the receiver MUST
ignore them. No padding is required following the last data element.

A **FILE_STREAM_INFORMATION** data element is as follows.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<p>0</p></th>

<th>3</th>

<th>5</th>

<td colspan="32">...</td>

<td colspan="32">StreamName (variable)</td>
</tr>

<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the byte offset from the beginning of this entry, at which the next
**FILE_STREAM_INFORMATION** entry is located, if multiple entries are
present in a buffer. This member is zero if no other entries follow this
one. An implementation MUST use this value to determine the location of
the next entry (if multiple entries are present in a buffer).

**StreamNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the length, in bytes, of the stream name string.

**StreamSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
size, in bytes, of the stream. The value of this field MUST be greater
than or equal to 0x0000000000000000.

**StreamAllocationSize (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the file stream allocation size, in bytes. The value of this
field MUST be an integer multiple of the
[**cluster**](#gt_feef37b3-c173-4f51-aab6-b55a6366259b) size.

**StreamName (variable):** A sequence of Unicode characters containing
the name of the stream using the form ":streamname:\$DATA", or
"::\$DATA" for the default data stream, as specified in section
[2.1.4](#Section_e2b19412a9254360b00986e3b8a020c8). This field is not
null-terminated and MUST be handled as a sequence of
**StreamNameLength** bytes.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before all of the stream information
could be returned. Only complete FILE_STREAM_INFORMATION structures are
returned.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.4.50 FileValidDataLengthInformation

This information class is used to set the valid data length information
for a file. A file's valid data length is the length, in bytes, of the
data that has been written to the file. This valid data extends from the
beginning of the file to the last byte in the file that has not been
zeroed or left uninitialized.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_157"
class="anchor"></span>[\<157\>](#Appendix_A_157)

A **FILE_VALID_DATA_LENGTH_INFORMATION** data element, defined as
follows, is provided by the client.

**ValidDataLength (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains the
new valid data length for the file. This parameter MUST be a positive
value that is greater than the current valid data length, but less than
or equal to the current file size.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table style="width:100%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 33%" />
<col style="width: 66%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_MEDIA_WRITE_PROTECTED</p>
<p>0xC00000A2</p></td>
<td>The target cannot be written to because it is write-protected.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER</p>
<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The <em>ValidDataLength</em> specified is not a valid parameter or
the given handle is to a sparse or compressed file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_PRIVILEGE_NOT_HELD</p>
<p>0xC0000061</p></td>
<td>The manage volume privilege is not held.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## 2.5 File System Information Classes

File system information classes are numerical values (specified by the
Level column in the following table) that specify what information on a
particular instance of a file system on a
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) is to be queried.
File system information classes can retrieve information such as the
file system type, volume label, size of the file system, and name of the
driver used to access the file system. The table indicates which file
system information classes are supported for query and set
operations.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_158"
class="anchor"></span>[\<158\>](#Appendix_A_158)

| File system information class | Level | Uses |
|----|----|----|
| [FileFsVolumeInformation](#Section_bf691378c34e4a13976e404ea1a87738) | 1 | Query |
| [FileFsLabelInformation](#Section_d5ab2652a7fd46559b35ea02bd05c0d8) | 2 | LOCAL<span id="Appendix_A_Target_159" class="anchor"></span>[\<159\>](#Appendix_A_159) |
| [FileFsSizeInformation](#Section_e13e068ce3a74dd494fd3892b492e6e7) | 3 | Query |
| [FileFsDeviceInformation](#Section_616b66d5b3354e1c8f87b4a55e8d3e4a) | 4 | Query |
| [FileFsAttributeInformation](#Section_ebc7e6e546504e54b17ccf60f6fbeeaa) | 5 | Query |
| [FileFsControlInformation](#Section_e5a707387ee446d9a5f76644daa49a51) | 6 | Query, Set |
| [FileFsFullSizeInformation](#Section_63768db7901242098cca00781e7322f5) | 7 | Query |
| [FileFsObjectIdInformation](#Section_dbf535ae315a45088bc584276ea106d4) | 8 | Query, Set |
| [FileFsDriverPathInformation](#Section_c530e7694b8744f4a188b9af8305eee0) | 9 | LOCAL<span id="Appendix_A_Target_160" class="anchor"></span>[\<160\>](#Appendix_A_160) |
| FileFsVolumeFlagsInformation | 10 | LOCAL<span id="Appendix_A_Target_161" class="anchor"></span>[\<161\>](#Appendix_A_161) |
| [FileFsSectorSizeInformation](#Section_3e75d97f1d0b4e47b43573c513837a57) | 11 | Query |

If an Information Class is specified that does not match the usage in
the above table, STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS MUST be returned. If a file
system does not implement one of the above defined uses of an
Information Class, STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER MUST be returned.

### 2.5.1 FileFsAttributeInformation

This information class is used to query attribute information for a file
system.

A **FILE_FS_ATTRIBUTE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is

**FileSystemAttributes (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains a bitmask of flags that specify attributes of the specified
file system as a combination of the following flags. The value of this
field MUST be a bitwise OR of zero or more of the following with the
exception that FILE_FILE_COMPRESSION and FILE_VOLUME_IS_COMPRESSED
cannot both be set. Any flag values not explicitly mentioned here can be
set to any value, and MUST be ignored.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_162"
class="anchor"></span>[\<162\>](#Appendix_A_162)

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 39%" />
<col style="width: 58%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_USN_JOURNAL</p>
<p>0x02000000</p></td>
<td>The file system implements a <a
href="#gt_01936446-8739-4b98-b83f-fb5e2a53ce4c"><strong>USN</strong></a>
change journal.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_OPEN_BY_FILE_ID</p>
<p>0x01000000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports opening a file by FileID or ObjectID.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_EXTENDED_ATTRIBUTES</p>
<p>0x00800000</p></td>
<td>The file system persistently stores Extended Attribute information
per file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_HARD_LINKS</p>
<p>0x00400000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports hard linking files.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_TRANSACTIONS</p>
<p>0x00200000</p></td>
<td>The <a
href="#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c"><strong>volume</strong></a>
supports transactions.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_163"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_163">&lt;163&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SEQUENTIAL_WRITE_ONCE</p>
<p>0x00100000</p></td>
<td>The underlying volume is write once.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_READ_ONLY_VOLUME</p>
<p>0x00080000</p></td>
<td>If set, the volume has been mounted in read-only mode.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_NAMED_STREAMS</p>
<p>0x00040000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports <a
href="#gt_03d3ec14-995b-486a-8938-e2a73f6de7e1"><strong>named
streams</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_ENCRYPTION</p>
<p>0x00020000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports the Encrypted File System (EFS).<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_164" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_164">&lt;164&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_OBJECT_IDS</p>
<p>0x00010000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports <a
href="#gt_aaaf2f1a-0b0a-487e-a0f0-c3510a6091b2"><strong>object
identifiers</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VOLUME_IS_COMPRESSED</p>
<p>0x00008000</p></td>
<td>The specified volume is a compressed volume. This flag is
incompatible with the FILE_FILE_COMPRESSION flag.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_POSIX_UNLINK_RENAME</p>
<p>0x00000400</p></td>
<td>The file system supports POSIX-style delete and rename
operations.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_165" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_165">&lt;165&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_RETURNS_CLEANUP_RESULT_INFO</p>
<p>0x00000200</p></td>
<td>On a successful cleanup operation, the file system returns
information that describes additional actions taken during cleanup, such
as deleting the file. File system filters can examine this information
in their post-cleanup callback.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_166"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_166">&lt;166&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_REMOTE_STORAGE</p>
<p>0x00000100</p></td>
<td>The file system supports remote storage.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_167" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_167">&lt;167&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_REPARSE_POINTS</p>
<p>0x00000080</p></td>
<td>The file system supports <a
href="#gt_4fed0b53-5fc8-4818-886f-93d87f3035e1"><strong>reparse
points</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_SPARSE_FILES</p>
<p>0x00000040</p></td>
<td>The file system supports <a
href="#gt_57c7faf0-6002-4b1a-b8fc-fc9244d5a6e5"><strong>sparse
files</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VOLUME_QUOTAS</p>
<p>0x00000020</p></td>
<td>The file system supports per-user quotas.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_FILE_COMPRESSION</p>
<p>0x00000010</p></td>
<td>The file volume supports file-based compression. This flag is
incompatible with the FILE_VOLUME_IS_COMPRESSED flag.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PERSISTENT_ACLS</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>The file system preserves and enforces access control lists
(ACLs).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_UNICODE_ON_DISK</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>The file system supports Unicode in file and directory names. This
flag applies only to file and directory names; the file system neither
restricts nor interprets the bytes of data within a file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_CASE_PRESERVED_NAMES</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>The file system preserves the case of file names when it places a
name on disk.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_CASE_SENSITIVE_SEARCH</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>The file system supports case-sensitive file names when looking up
(searching for) file names in a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORT_INTEGRITY_STREAMS</p>
<p>0x04000000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports integrity streams.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_BLOCK_REFCOUNTING</p>
<p>0x08000000</p></td>
<td>The file system supports sharing logical clusters between files on
the same volume. The file system reallocates on writes to shared
clusters. Indicates that FSCTL_DUPLICATE_EXTENTS_TO_FILE is a supported
operation.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_SUPPORTS_SPARSE_VDL</p>
<p>0x10000000</p></td>
<td><p>The file system tracks whether each cluster of a file contains
valid data (either from explicit file writes or automatic zeros) or
invalid data (has not yet been written to or zeroed).</p>
<p>File systems that use Sparse VDL do not store a valid data length
(section <a href="#Section_5c9f9d50f0e040b19b840b78f59158b1">2.4.50</a>)
and do not require that valid data be contiguous within a file.</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**MaximumComponentNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit signed integer that
contains the maximum [**file name
component**](#gt_fbbda8a1-53a1-4637-b0c9-8d04616913d5) length, in
characters, supported by the specified file system. The value of this
field MUST be greater than zero and MUST be no more than
255.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_168"
class="anchor"></span>[\<168\>](#Appendix_A_168)

**FileSystemNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the length, in bytes, of the file system name in the
**FileSystemName** field. The value of this field MUST be greater than
0.

**FileSystemName (variable):** A variable-length Unicode field
containing the name of the file system. This field is not
null-terminated and MUST be handled as a sequence of
**FileSystemNameLength** bytes. This field is intended to be informative
only. A client SHOULD NOT infer file system type specific behavior from
this field.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_169"
class="anchor"></span>[\<169\>](#Appendix_A_169)

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before all of the file system
information could be returned; only a portion of the FileSystemName
field is returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.2 FileFsControlInformation

This information class is used to query or set quota and content
indexing control information for a file system
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c).

Setting quota information requires the caller to have permission to open
a volume handle or a handle to the quota index
file<span id="Appendix_A_Target_170"
class="anchor"></span>[\<170\>](#Appendix_A_170) for write access.

A **FILE_FS_CONTROL_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server or provided by the client.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />

<td colspan="32">FreeSpaceStartFiltering</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FreeSpaceThreshold</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FreeSpaceStopFiltering</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">DefaultQuotaThreshold</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">DefaultQuotaLimit</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileSystemControlFlags</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Padding</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**FreeSpaceStartFiltering (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the minimum amount of free disk space, in bytes, that is
required for the operating system's [**content indexing
service**](#gt_f83c4971-a0c2-41a5-af59-6fae035ecb4a) to begin document
filtering. This value SHOULD be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.

**FreeSpaceThreshold (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that contains
the minimum amount of free disk space, in bytes, that is required for
the indexing service to continue to filter documents and merge word
lists. This value SHOULD be set to 0 and MUST be ignored.

**FreeSpaceStopFiltering (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the minimum amount of free disk space, in bytes, that is
required for the content indexing service to continue filtering. This
value SHOULD be set to 0, and MUST be ignored.

**DefaultQuotaThreshold (8 bytes):** A 64-bit unsigned integer that
contains the default per-user [**disk
quota**](#gt_82dfddec-0095-4f51-9b36-b6f5a0458e22) warning threshold, in
bytes, for the volume. A value of 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF specifies that no
default quota warning threshold per user is set.

**DefaultQuotaLimit (8 bytes):** A 64-bit unsigned integer that contains
the default per-user disk quota limit, in bytes, for the volume. A value
of 0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF specifies that no default quota limit per user is
set.

**FileSystemControlFlags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains a bitmask of flags that control quota enforcement and logging
of user-related quota events on the volume. The following bit flags are
valid in any combination. Bits not defined in the following table SHOULD
be set to 0, and MUST be ignored.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_171"
class="anchor"></span>[\<171\>](#Appendix_A_171)

<table style="width:98%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 35%" />
<col style="width: 62%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_CONTENT_INDEX_DISABLED</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>Content indexing is disabled.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_LOG_QUOTA_LIMIT</p>
<p>0x00000020</p></td>
<td>An event log entry will be created when the user exceeds the
assigned disk quota limit.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_LOG_QUOTA_THRESHOLD</p>
<p>0x00000010</p></td>
<td>An event log entry will be created when the user exceeds his or her
assigned quota warning threshold.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_LOG_VOLUME_LIMIT</p>
<p>0x00000080</p></td>
<td>An event log entry will be created when the volume's free space
limit is exceeded.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_LOG_VOLUME_THRESHOLD</p>
<p>0x00000040</p></td>
<td>An event log entry will be created when the volume's free space
threshold is exceeded.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td><p>Quotas are tracked and enforced on the volume.</p>
<p>Note: FILE_VC_QUOTA_TRACK takes precedence over this flag. In other
words, if both FILE_VC_QUOTA_TRACK and FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE are set,
the FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE flag is ignored. This flag will be ignored if
a client attempts to set it.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_QUOTA_TRACK</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td><p>Quotas are tracked on the volume, but they are not enforced.
Tracked quotas enable reporting on the file system space used by system
users. If both this flag and FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE are specified,
FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE is ignored.</p>
<p>Note: This flag takes precedence over FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE. In other
words, if both FILE_VC_QUOTA_TRACK and FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE are set,
the FILE_VC_QUOTA_ENFORCE flag is ignored. This flag will be ignored if
a client attempts to set it.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_QUOTAS_INCOMPLETE</p>
<p>0x00000100</p></td>
<td><p>The quota information for the volume is incomplete because it is
corrupt, or the system is in the process of rebuilding the quota
information.</p>
<p>Note: This does not necessarily imply that FILE_VC_QUOTAS_REBUILDING
is set. This flag will be ignored if a client attempts to set
it.</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VC_QUOTAS_REBUILDING</p>
<p>0x00000200</p></td>
<td><p>The file system is rebuilding the quota information for the
volume.</p>
<p>Note: This does not necessarily imply that FILE_VC_QUOTAS_INCOMPLETE
is set. This flag will be ignored if a client attempts to set
it.</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Padding (4 bytes):** This field SHOULD be set to 0x00000000 and MUST
be ignored.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_VOLUME_NOT_UPGRADED</p>
<p>0xC000029C</p></td>
<td>The file system on the volume does not support quotas.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**DriverInPath (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. Set to TRUE if
the driver is in the I/O path for the file system volume; set to FALSE
otherwise.

**Reserved (3 bytes):** Reserved for alignment. This field can contain
any value and MUST be ignored.

**DriverNameLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the length of the **DriverName** string.

**DriverName (variable):** A variable-length Unicode field containing
the name of the driver for which to query. This sequence of Unicode
characters MUST NOT be null-terminated.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />

<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**TotalAllocationUnits (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the total number of allocation units on the volume that are
available to the user associated with the calling thread. The value of
this field MUST be greater than or equal to
0.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_172"
class="anchor"></span>[\<172\>](#Appendix_A_172)

**CallerAvailableAllocationUnits (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer
that contains the total number of free allocation units on the volume
that are available to the user associated with the calling thread. The
value of this field MUST be greater than or equal to
0.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_173"
class="anchor"></span>[\<173\>](#Appendix_A_173)

**ActualAvailableAllocationUnits (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer
that contains the total number of free allocation units on the volume.
The value of this field MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**SectorsPerAllocationUnit (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the number of sectors in each allocation unit.

**BytesPerSector (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the number of bytes in each sector.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">VolumeLabelLength</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">VolumeLabel (variable)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">...</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**VolumeLabelLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the length, in bytes, including the trailing null, if present, of the
name for the volume.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_174"
class="anchor"></span>[\<174\>](#Appendix_A_174)

**VolumeLabel (variable):** A variable-length Unicode field containing
the name of the volume. The content of this field can be a
null-terminated string, or it can be a string padded with the space
character to be **VolumeLabelLength** bytes long.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.6 FileFsObjectIdInformation

class="anchor"></span>[\<175\>](#Appendix_A_175)

**ObjectId (16 bytes):** A 16-byte
[**GUID**](#gt_f49694cc-c350-462d-ab8e-816f0103c6c1) that identifies the
file system [**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c) on
the disk. This value is not required to be unique on the system.

**ExtendedInfo (48 bytes):** A 48-byte value containing extended
information on the file system volume. If no extended information has
been written for this file system volume, the server MUST return 48
bytes of 0x00 in this field.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_176"
class="anchor"></span>[\<176\>](#Appendix_A_176)

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

</tr>

<p>0xC000000D</p></td>
<td>The file system does not implement object IDs.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.7 FileFsSectorSizeInformation

This information class is used to query for the extended sector size and
alignment information for a volume. The message contains a
**FILE_FS_SECTOR_SIZE_INFORMATION** data
element.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_177"
class="anchor"></span>[\<177\>](#Appendix_A_177)

A **FILE_FS_SECTOR_SIZE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows,
is returned to the caller.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

</colgroup>

<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>

<th>6</th>

</tr>
