<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">LogicalBytesPerSector</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">PhysicalBytesPerSectorForAtomicity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">PhysicalBytesPerSectorForPerformance</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td
colspan="32">FileSystemEffectivePhysicalBytesPerSectorForAtomicity</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Flags</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">ByteOffsetForSectorAlignment</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">ByteOffsetForPartitionAlignment</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**LogicalBytesPerSector (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the number of bytes in a logical sector for the device backing
the volume. This field is the unit of logical addressing for the device
and is not the unit of atomic write. Applications SHOULD NOT utilize
this value for operations requiring physical sector alignment.

**PhysicalBytesPerSectorForAtomicity (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned
integer that contains the number of bytes in a physical sector for the
device backing the volume. Note that this is the reported physical
sector size of the device and is the unit of atomic write. Applications
SHOULD<span id="Appendix_A_Target_178"
class="anchor"></span>[\<178\>](#Appendix_A_178) utilize this value for
operations requiring sector alignment.

**PhysicalBytesPerSectorForPerformance (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned
integer that contains the number of bytes in a physical sector for the
device backing the volume. This is the reported physical sector size of
the device and is the unit of performance. Applications
SHOULD<span id="Appendix_A_Target_179"
class="anchor"></span>[\<179\>](#Appendix_A_179) utilize this value for
operations requiring sector alignment.

**FileSystemEffectivePhysicalBytesPerSectorForAtomicity (4 bytes):** A
32-bit unsigned integer containing the unit, in bytes, that the file
system on the volume will use for internal operations that require
alignment and atomicity.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_180"
class="anchor"></span>[\<180\>](#Appendix_A_180)

**Flags (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that indicates the flags
for this operation. Currently defined flags are:

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 46%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>SSINFO_FLAGS_ALIGNED_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>When set, this flag indicates that the first physical sector of the
device is aligned with the first logical sector. When not set, the first
physical sector of the device is misaligned with the first logical
sector.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>SSINFO_FLAGS_PARTITION_ALIGNED_ON_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>When set, this flag indicates that the partition is aligned to
physical sector boundaries on the storage device.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>SSINFO_FLAGS_NO_SEEK_PENALTY</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>When set, the device reports that it does not incur a seek penalty
(this typically indicates that the device does not have rotating media,
such as flash-based disks).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>SSINFO_FLAGS_TRIM_ENABLED</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>When set, the device supports TRIM operations, either T13 (ATA) TRIM
or T10 (SCSI/SAS) UNMAP.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**ByteOffsetForSectorAlignment (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer
that contains the logical sector offset within the first physical sector
where the first logical sector is placed, in bytes. If this value is set
to SSINFO_OFFSET_UNKNOWN (0XFFFFFFFF), there was insufficient
information to compute this field.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_181"
class="anchor"></span>[\<181\>](#Appendix_A_181)

**ByteOffsetForPartitionAlignment (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer
that contains the byte offset from the first physical sector where the
first partition is placed. If this value is set to SSINFO_OFFSET_UNKNOWN
(0XFFFFFFFF), there was either insufficient information or an error was
encountered in computing this field.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error Code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.8 FileFsSizeInformation

This information class is used to query
[**sector**](#gt_b5bbe646-aa5f-4b4e-ae9e-bdae444cbfa2) size information
for a file system
[**volume**](#gt_9a876829-33a1-4f0b-8b81-8552b7e5561c).

A **FILE_FS_SIZE_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is
returned by the server.

**TotalAllocationUnits (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the total number of allocation units on the volume that are
available to the user associated with the calling thread. This value
MUST be greater than or equal to 0.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_182"
class="anchor"></span>[\<182\>](#Appendix_A_182)

**AvailableAllocationUnits (8 bytes):** A 64-bit signed integer that
contains the total number of free allocation units on the volume that
are available to the user associated with the calling thread. This value
MUST be greater than or equal to 0.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_183"
class="anchor"></span>[\<183\>](#Appendix_A_183)

**SectorsPerAllocationUnit (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the number of sectors in each allocation unit.

**BytesPerSector (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the number of bytes in each sector.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>

<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>

<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.9 FileFsVolumeInformation

This information class is used to query information on a

A **FILE_FS_VOLUME_INFORMATION** data element, defined as follows, is

**VolumeCreationTime (8 bytes):** The time when the volume was created;
see section [2.1.1](#Section_a69cc039d28846739598772b6083f8bf). The
value of this field MUST be greater than or equal to 0.

**VolumeSerialNumber (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that
contains the serial number of the volume. The serial number is an opaque
value generated by the file system at format time, and is not
necessarily related to any hardware serial number for the device on
which the file system is located. No specific format or content of this
field is required for protocol interoperation. This value is not
required to be unique.

**VolumeLabelLength (4 bytes):** A 32-bit unsigned integer that contains
the length, in bytes, including the trailing null, if present, of the
name of the volume.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_184"
class="anchor"></span>[\<184\>](#Appendix_A_184)

**SupportsObjects (1 byte):** A [Boolean (section
2.1.8)](#Section_8ce7b38cd3cc415dab39944000ea77ff) value. Set to TRUE if
the file system supports [**object-oriented file
system**](#gt_fbc5b8e4-ba0b-497d-ab17-1cfc3f81554c) objects; set to
FALSE otherwise.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_185"
class="anchor"></span>[\<185\>](#Appendix_A_185)

**Reserved (1 byte):** An 8-bit field. This field is reserved. This
field MUST be set to zero and MUST be ignored.

**VolumeLabel (variable):** A variable-length Unicode field containing
the name of the volume. The content of this field can be a
null-terminated string or can be a string padded with the space
character to be **VolumeLabelLength** bytes long.

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

If the volume label is greater than 32 characters, return the first 32
characters of the label and STATUS_SUCCESS.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW</p>
<p>0x80000005</p></td>
<td>The output buffer was filled before all of the volume information
could be returned; only a portion of the <strong>VolumeLabel</strong>
field is returned.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

### 2.5.10 FileFsDeviceInformation

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">DeviceType</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Characteristics</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**DeviceType (4 bytes):** This identifies the type of given volume. It
MUST be one of the following.

<table style="width:51%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 22%" />
<col style="width: 28%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_DEVICE_CD_ROM</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>Volume resides on a CD ROM.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_DEVICE_DISK</p>
<p>0x00000007</p></td>
<td>Volume resides on a disk.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**Characteristics (4 bytes):** A bit field which identifies various
characteristics about a given volume. The following are valid bit
values.

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 47%" />
<col style="width: 49%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_REMOVABLE_MEDIA</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the storage device supports removable media. Notice
that this characteristic indicates removable media, not a removable
device. For example, drivers for JAZ drive devices specify this
characteristic, but drivers for PCMCIA flash disks do not.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_READ_ONLY_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the device cannot be written to.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_FLOPPY_DISKETTE</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the device is a floppy disk device.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_WRITE_ONCE_MEDIA</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the device supports write-once media.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_REMOTE_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00000010</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the volume is for a remote file system like SMB or
CIFS.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_DEVICE_IS_MOUNTED</p>
<p>0x00000020</p></td>
<td>Indicates that a file system is mounted on the device.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_VIRTUAL_VOLUME</p>
<p>0x00000040</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the volume does not directly reside on storage media
but resides on some other type of media (memory for example).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_DEVICE_SECURE_OPEN</p>
<p>0x00000100</p></td>
<td>By default, volumes do not check the ACL associated with the volume,
but instead use the ACLs associated with individual files on the volume.
When this flag is set the volume ACL is also checked.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_CHARACTERISTIC_TS_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00001000</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the device object is part of a Terminal Services
device stack. See <a
href="%5bMS-RDPBCGR%5d.pdf#Section_5073f4ed1e9345e1b0396e30c385867c">[MS-RDPBCGR]</a>
for more information.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_CHARACTERISTIC_WEBDAV_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x00002000</p></td>
<td>Indicates that a web-based Distributed Authoring and Versioning
(WebDAV) file system is mounted on the device. See <a
href="%5bMS-WDVME%5d.pdf#Section_8cafdf55ee5c443ebdb72cb2ab1fb2c3">[MS-WDVME]</a>
for more information.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_DEVICE_ALLOW_APPCONTAINER_TRAVERSAL</p>
<p>0x00020000</p></td>
<td>The IO Manager normally performs a full security check for traverse
access on every file open when the client is an appcontainer. Setting of
this flag bypasses this enforced traverse access check if the client
token already has traverse privileges.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_186"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_186">&lt;186&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_PORTABLE_DEVICE</p>
<p>0x0004000</p></td>
<td>Indicates that the given device resides on a portable bus like USB
or Firewire and that the entire device (not just the media) can be
removed from the system.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

This operation returns a status code as specified in section
[2.2](#Section_a7527db8cbfe4f2aabc3133bf5160bd7). Upon success, the
status code returned by the function that processes this file system
information class is STATUS_SUCCESS. The most common error codes are
listed in the following table.

<table>
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 32%" />
<col style="width: 67%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Error code</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>STATUS_INFO_LENGTH_MISMATCH</p>
<p>0xC0000004</p></td>
<td>The specified information record length does not match the length
that is required for the specified information class.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## 2.6 File Attributes

The following attributes are defined for files and directories. They can
be used in any combination unless noted in the description of the
attribute's meaning. There is no file attribute with the value
0x00000000 because a value of 0x00000000 in the **FileAttributes** field
means that the file attributes for this file MUST NOT be changed when
setting basic information for the file.

**Note**: File systems silently ignore any attribute that is not
supported by that file system. Unsupported attributes MUST NOT be
persisted on the media. It is recommended that unsupported attributes be
masked off when encountered.

<table style="width:100%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 41%" />
<col style="width: 58%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is read-only. For a file, applications can
read the file but cannot write to it or delete it. For a directory,
applications cannot delete it, but applications can create and delete
files from that directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_HIDDEN</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is hidden. Files and directories marked
with this attribute do not appear in an ordinary directory listing.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_SYSTEM</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that the operating system uses a part of or uses
exclusively.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_DIRECTORY</p>
<p>0x00000010</p></td>
<td>This item is a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_ARCHIVE</p>
<p>0x00000020</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that requires to be archived. Applications use
this attribute to mark files for backup or removal.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL</p>
<p>0x00000080</p></td>
<td><p>A file that does not have other attributes set. This flag is used
to clear all other flags by specifying it with no other flags set.</p>
<p>This flag MUST be ignored if other flags are set.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_187" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_187">&lt;187&gt;</a></p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_TEMPORARY</p>
<p>0x00000100</p></td>
<td>A file that is being used for temporary storage. The operating
system can choose to store this file's data in memory rather than on
mass storage, writing the data to mass storage only if data remains in
the file when the file is closed.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_SPARSE_FILE</p>
<p>0x00000200</p></td>
<td>A file that is a <a
href="#gt_57c7faf0-6002-4b1a-b8fc-fc9244d5a6e5"><strong>sparse
file</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_REPARSE_POINT</p>
<p>0x00000400</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that has an associated <a
href="#gt_4fed0b53-5fc8-4818-886f-93d87f3035e1"><strong>reparse
point</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_COMPRESSED</p>
<p>0x00000800</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is compressed. For a file, all of the data
in the file is compressed. For a directory, compression is the default
for newly created files and subdirectories.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_OFFLINE</p>
<p>0x00001000</p></td>
<td>The data in this file is not available immediately. This attribute
indicates that the file data is physically moved to offline storage.
This attribute is used by Remote Storage, which is hierarchical storage
management software.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NOT_CONTENT_INDEXED</p>
<p>0x00002000</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is not indexed by the <a
href="#gt_f83c4971-a0c2-41a5-af59-6fae035ecb4a"><strong>content indexing
service</strong></a>.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_ENCRYPTED</p>
<p>0x00004000</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is encrypted. For a file, all data streams
in the file are encrypted. For a directory, encryption is the default
for newly created files and subdirectories.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_INTEGRITY_STREAM</p>
<p>0x00008000</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is configured with integrity support. For a
file, all data streams in the file have integrity support. For a
directory, integrity support is the default for newly created files and
subdirectories, unless the caller specifies otherwise.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_188" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_188">&lt;188&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NO_SCRUB_DATA</p>
<p>0x00020000</p></td>
<td>A file or directory that is configured to be excluded from the data
integrity scan. For a directory configured with
FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NO_SCRUB_DATA, the default for newly created files and
subdirectories is to inherit the
<strong>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NO_SCRUB_DATA</strong> attribute.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_189" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_189">&lt;189&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_RECALL_ON_OPEN</p>
<p>0x00040000</p></td>
<td>This attribute appears only in directory enumeration classes
(FILE_DIRECTORY_INFORMATION, FILE_BOTH_DIR_INFORMATION, etc.). When this
attribute is set, it means that the file or directory has no physical
representation on the local system; the item is virtual. Opening the
item will be more expensive than usual because it will cause at least
some of the file or directory content to be fetched from a remote store.
This attribute can only be set by kernel-mode components. This attribute
is for use with hierarchical storage management software.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_190" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_190">&lt;190&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_PINNED</p>
<p>0x00080000</p></td>
<td>This attribute indicates user intent that the file or directory
should be kept fully present locally even when not being actively
accessed. This attribute is for use with hierarchical storage management
software.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_191" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_191">&lt;191&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_UNPINNED</p>
<p>0x00100000</p></td>
<td>This attribute indicates that the file or directory should not be
kept fully present locally except when being actively accessed. This
attribute is for use with hierarchical storage management software.<span
id="Appendix_A_Target_192" class="anchor"></span><a
href="#Appendix_A_192">&lt;192&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ATTRIBUTE_RECALL_ON_DATA_ACCESS</p>
<p>0x00400000</p></td>
<td>When this attribute is set, it means that the file or directory is
not fully present locally. For a file this means that not all of its
data is on local storage (for example, it may be sparse with some data
still in remote storage). For a directory it means that some of the
directory contents are being virtualized from another location. Reading
the file or enumerating the directory will be more expensive than usual
because it will cause at least some of the file or directory content to
be fetched from a remote store. Only kernel-mode callers can set this
attribute. This attribute is for use with hierarchical storage
management software.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_193"
class="anchor"></span><a href="#Appendix_A_193">&lt;193&gt;</a></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## 2.7 Directory Change Notifications

The following definitions are part of the Directory Change Notification
algorithm defined in
[\[MS-FSA\]](%5bMS-FSA%5d.pdf#Section_860b1516c45247b4bdbc625d344e2041)
section 2.1.5.11.

### 2.7.1 FILE_NOTIFY_INFORMATION

The **FILE_NOTIFY_INFORMATION** structure contains the changes for which
the client is being notified. The structure consists of the following.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>1</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">NextEntryOffset</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">Action</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileNameLength</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">FileName (variable)</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**NextEntryOffset (4 bytes):** The offset, in bytes, from the beginning
of this structure to the subsequent **FILE_NOTIFY_INFORMATION**
structure. If there are no subsequent structures, the
**NextEntryOffset** field MUST be 0. **NextEntryOffset** MUST always be
an integral multiple of 4. The **FileName** array MUST be padded to the
next 4-byte boundary counted from the beginning of the structure.

**Action (4 bytes):** The changes that occurred on the file. This field
MUST contain one of the following
values.<span id="Appendix_A_Target_194"
class="anchor"></span>[\<194\>](#Appendix_A_194)

<table style="width:97%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 39%" />
<col style="width: 57%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>Value</th>
<th>Meaning</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_ADDED</p>
<p>0x00000001</p></td>
<td>The file was renamed, and <strong>FileName</strong> contains the new
name. This notification is only sent when the rename operation changes
the directory the file resides in. The client will also receive a
FILE_ACTION_REMOVED notification. This notification will not be received
if the file is renamed within a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_REMOVED</p>
<p>0x00000002</p></td>
<td>The file was renamed, and <strong>FileName</strong> contains the old
name. This notification is only sent when the rename operation changes
the directory the file resides in. The client will also receive a
FILE_ACTION_ADDED notification. This notification will not be received
if the file is renamed within a directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_MODIFIED</p>
<p>0x00000003</p></td>
<td>The file was modified. This can be a change to the data or
attributes of the file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_RENAMED_OLD_NAME</p>
<p>0x00000004</p></td>
<td>The file was renamed, and <strong>FileName</strong> contains the old
name. This notification is only sent when the rename operation does not
change the directory the file resides in. The client will also receive a
FILE_ACTION_RENAMED_NEW_NAME notification. This notification will not be
received if the file is renamed to a different directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_RENAMED_NEW_NAME</p>
<p>0x00000005</p></td>
<td>The file was renamed, and <strong>FileName</strong> contains the new
name. This notification is only sent when the rename operation does not
change the directory the file resides in. The client will also receive a
FILE_ACTION_RENAMED_OLD_NAME notification. This notification will not be
received if the file is renamed to a different directory.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_ADDED_STREAM</p>
<p>0x00000006</p></td>
<td>The file was added to a named stream.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_REMOVED_STREAM</p>
<p>0x00000007</p></td>
<td>The file was removed from the named stream.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_MODIFIED_STREAM</p>
<p>0x00000008</p></td>
<td>The file was modified. This can be a change to the data or
attributes of the file.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_REMOVED_BY_DELETE</p>
<p>0x00000009</p></td>
<td><p>An object ID was removed because the file the object ID referred
to was deleted.</p>
<p>This notification is only sent when the directory being monitored is
the special directory "\$Extend\$ObjId:$O:$INDEX_ALLOCATION".</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_ID_NOT_TUNNELLED</p>
<p>0x0000000A</p></td>
<td><p>An attempt to tunnel object ID information to a file being
created or renamed failed because the object ID is in use by another
file on the same volume.</p>
<p>This notification is only sent when the directory being monitored is
the special directory "\$Extend\$ObjId:$O:$INDEX_ALLOCATION".</p></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><p>FILE_ACTION_TUNNELLED_ID_COLLISION</p>
<p>0x0000000B</p></td>
<td><p>An attempt to tunnel object ID information to a file being
renamed failed because the file already has an object ID.</p>
<p>This notification is only sent when the directory being monitored is
the special directory "\$Extend\$ObjId:$O:$INDEX_ALLOCATION".</p></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

If two or more files have been renamed, the corresponding
**FILE_NOTIFY_INFORMATION** entries for each file rename MUST be
consecutive in this response for the client to make the correct
correspondence between old and new names.

**FileNameLength (4 bytes):** The length, in bytes, of the file name in
the **FileName** field.

**FileName (variable):** A Unicode string with the name of the file that
changed.

## 2.8 Cluster Shared Volume File System IOCTLs

SQL Server Remote Storage Profile
[\[MS-SQLRS\]](%5bMS-SQLRS%5d.pdf#Section_3a7a8a09b876465d99b4de0e84d8f101)
relies on the [**I/O control
(IOCTL)**](#gt_09d6bc87-34ed-48e8-b4d4-962e90543462) code structures,
and definitions in this section, to interpret certain fields that can be
sent or received as part of its processing. See section
[2.3](#Section_4dc027799d9543f8bba48d4ce4961458) for more information
about processing.

### 2.8.1 IOCTL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY Request

The IOCTL_STORAGE_QUERY_PROPERTY Request message requests that the
server return the properties of a storage device or verify that the
request is supported.

<table style="width:95%;">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />

<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
<col style="width: 2%" />
</colgroup>
<thead>
<tr>
<th>0</th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>

<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>

<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>2</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
<th>2</th>
<th>3</th>
<th>4</th>
<th>5</th>
<th>6</th>
<th>7</th>
<th>8</th>
<th>9</th>
<th><p>3</p>
<p>0</p></th>
<th>1</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">PropertyId</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="32">QueryType</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

**PropertyId (4 bytes):** This field MUST be set to 0x00000006.

**QueryType (4 bytes):** Contains flags indicating the type of query to
be performed.

**Version (4 bytes):** Contains the size of this structure, in bytes.

**Size (4 bytes):** Specifies the total size of the data returned, in
bytes.

**BytesPerCacheLine (4 bytes):** The number of bytes in a cache line of
the device.

**BytesOffsetForCacheAlignment (4 bytes):** The address offset necessary
for proper cache access alignment, in bytes.

**BytesPerLogicalSector (4 bytes):** The number of bytes in a logical
sector of the device.

**BytesPerPhysicalSector (4 bytes):** The number of bytes in a physical
sector of the device.

**BytesOffsetForSectorAlignment (4 bytes):** The logical sector offset
within the first physical sector where the first logical sector is
placed, in bytes.

### 2.8.3 IOCTL_VOLUME_GET_GPT_ATTRIBUTES Request

The IOCTL_VOLUME_GET_GPT_ATTRIBUTES Request message retrieves the
attributes for a volume.

This message does not contain any additional data elements.

### 2.8.4 IOCTL_VOLUME_GET_GPT_ATTRIBUTES Reply

of the volume.
